the rapporteur. the european commission's proposal under consideration concerns the development of space policy, no longer at the level of individual member states but at the level of the european union. it was drawn up jointly with the european space agency and proposes three lines of action: strengthening the base of space activities, improving scientific knowledge and delivering benefits for markets and society. the report by the committee on industry, external trade, research and energy welcomes these lines of action and recognises the overall benefit that will be derived from the development of space activities. the development of launch systems is a necessary prerequisite for any other space activity. in this area we can build on the tradition and know-how of european countries, which has been developed over the last few decades, particularly with the ariane launch system. the second line of action comes naturally, since space is an ideal place to conduct research that cannot be done on earth. for example, astronomical research into radiation that does not penetrate the earth's atmosphere, research under conditions of zero gravity, etc. the third axis is particularly broad and includes all of what we call space applications. they range from satellite telecommunications to navigation and surveying the earth and have become an integral part of our daily lives. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's space programme is not disrupted by the threat of nuclear accidents. it is particularly important, in my view, that the commission's report stresses in the very first paragraph that space activities must serve exclusively peaceful purposes. i am particularly concerned, mr president, to note that an amendment has been tabled which proposes to add the phrase 'whose purposes may include military applications in the context of peacekeeping operations'. the adoption of this amendment opens the way to any military activity in space, provided it is disguised as a 'peace operation', and we have seen quite a few of these phenomena on earth in recent years. in short, it's a trojan horse that will alter the character of the exhibition and open the coffers of hell. i would ask all colleagues to support the commission's text. i would also suggest that they adopt amendments nos 2, 3 and 4, which are drawn from the opinion of mr souladakis. i would stress, mr president, that a position against the militarization of space is of enormous importance, especially after the recent decision of the united states government to withdraw from the anti-ballistic missile treaty and its insistence on developing the so-called missile shield. i have tabled amendment no 5 to record the consequences of this action. let me also point out that in my personal opinion some aspects of the proposed gmes programme involve military activities. the implementation of space policy is also the subject of our discussion. the european space agency has been operating on a transnational basis on our continent for several years and has played a key role in the development of space activities in european countries. it is therefore quite natural that there should be close cooperation between the european union and the european space agency, a cooperation which has already borne fruit. the commission report notes the role of the european space agency and proposes that in the long term it should join the european union while maintaining its autonomy. let me emphasize that this, personally, does not agree with me. i fear that if this happens, the european space agency will lose the autonomy that has helped it to emerge as a world leader. international cooperation is of course essential in the field of space and the commission's report rightly speaks favourably of it, stressing the need to develop it not only with the russian federation, the united states, china and japan, but also with less developed countries to which the european union could offer access to space. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. it is well known that access to space is both lucrative and expensive. the sixth framework programme for research funding sets out space and aeronautics as one of the priority areas. this shows good intentions, but it is not enough. a more general commitment would be both desirable and necessary. the commission has already taken a number of initiatives in this area, including the creation of a european space agency, the establishment of the rapporteur. - mr president, madam vice-president of the commission, when i started working on this report i thought it would take less time. turns out, it was a little more complicated than i thought. in any case, today we are heading for a happy ending, and that is what matters. the aim of the proposal for a directive is to harmonise the maximum length of both rigid and articulated buses for transport throughout the european union. the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to carcinogens at work. there was simply the guarantee that rigid buses up to 12 metres and articulated buses up to 18 metres would be able to circulate freely in the european union. for example, for rigid buses we have four different maximum lengths at national level, with the result that some buses are unsuitable for use throughout the european union and thus distort competition. the member states wished to harmonise these maximum lengths, at least for buses used throughout the european union, and asked the commission to draw up a proposal. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. in the first amendment, we proposed to extend the period during which member states may allow the circulation on their territory of buses with dimensions not complying with the requirements of the new directive from december 2009, as proposed by the commission, to 2015, in order to allow a smooth economic life cycle, so to speak, of these buses. the council further extended this deadline until december 2020, an amendment to which the committee on regional policy, transport and tourism fully agreed. the second amendment by the european parliament proposed that articulated buses should be the same length as buses with trailers, i.e. 18.75 metres. the common position accepted this amendment too, and of course there is no problem with that. the last amendment allowed rigid buses up to 13.5 metres in length to have two axles. buses between 13,5 metres and 15 metres in length should have three axles to avoid exceeding the maximum weight per axle. and that amendment was accepted. the only part of it that was not accepted was the concept of a rigid bus, but it is merely a matter of terminology. the common position endorses the commission's proposal to use the new criteria for the margin of manoeuvre, which are currently being approved by the united nations economic commission for europe in geneva. i would remind you that portugal and the united kingdom may, for a period of up to three years from the entry into force of the directive, ban on their territory buses which do not meet the old and stricter criteria of freedom of manoeuvre. member states may also restrict the use of long-distance buses on a local basis for safety or other reasons. it is the article 7 of the common position which has been discussed at length in our committee. this is considered to cover to a large extent the concerns expressed in certain amendments rejected at second reading in the committee on regional policy, transport and tourism. it is therefore clear that after all this i can do nothing but propose what i have proposed to the commission, namely that the house should adopt this common position without amendments, since it strikes a reasonable balance between the internal market, the regulatory framework, safety, technical and commercial aspects.

rapporteur. – mr president, i have given my opinion in my capacity as rapporteur on the amendments tabled to my report. at this point, however, i should like to inform the house that if amendment no 1 – which, in my opinion, completely changes the spirit of the report as adopted by the committee on industry, external trade, research and energy – is adopted then i will not be able to support the final resolution and i will be in the unpleasant position of voting against my own report.
in writing. - (el) it is not common for the rapporteur to vote against his own report. in the present case, the spirit of the report, as adopted by the committee on industry, foreign trade, research and energy, has been so distorted that, in keeping with my political convictions, i had no choice but to reject it. i consider it very important that the committee on industry, foreign trade, research and energy has adopted by a majority the view that space activities should be used exclusively for peaceful purposes. it would have been even more important if the plenary of the european parliament had retained the substance of this thesis. however, the adoption of the amendment which added that these purposes could include military use for peacekeeping operations totally neutralises this pacifist position. thus, with the primary responsibility of the group of the european people's party, which proposed this amendment, and the co-responsibility of the group of the party of european socialists, which supported it, the european parliament opens the way to any military activity in space, provided that it is carried out under the cover of a 'peacekeeping operation'. we have seen many times, and we are seeing it more and more often, how the eu leaders conceive of peacekeeping operations. we saw it in yugoslavia and in the balkans, we see it in afghanistan and we will see it again in every corner of the world. aggression, whatever the pretext, is at the heart of imperialism. it is clear that they now intend to use space to facilitate their criminal policy towards the people; they want to use it to gather information and to spy, and to better prepare their operations in the context of the 'european defence and security policy', in the context of the terrorist witch-hunt and possibly in other areas as well. some are attracted by the idea of working with the us on the 'missile shield', and perhaps some think that space would be the ideal place to put weapons. we are not surprised that the european parliament has preferred to express a militaristic rather than a pacifist vote, and this is not the first time either. we expect nothing less from an institution that the bourgeoisie has created to protect itself. the result of the vote does not mean that the question is closed for the popular movement. resistance to imperialist policies is growing worldwide, whether in the form of economic subjugation or military intervention. we have no doubt that the final outcome of the struggle of the peoples will be a different world, a peaceful human society designed for men.
in writing. - (el) the report mixes certain undeniable findings with other data which are only hypotheses, to finally draw conclusions which raise at the very least questions about the intentions of the rapporteur. for example, it is right to talk about the pollution of marine ecosystems, and therefore of fish, by dioxins, which can enter the food chain, just as it is right to talk about the risks posed by the consumption of genetically modified fish from third countries. however, despite this, no data are mentioned to support the thesis that there is over-exploitation of resources and risks to fish stocks. unfortunately, the report does not clearly identify the main culprit behind the problems mentioned above, which is none other than the large capital active in the fisheries and industrial sectors. it is responsible for the pollution of the seas by waste, dioxins and other factors, and, insensitive to the consequences for the environment, does not hesitate to use any production method to maximise its profit. it is the same motivation that drives capital to produce and release genetically modified organisms, fish, etc. into the environment. the large capital of the fishing industry - which operates in the sector with fishing fleets consisting of a large number of vessels of high tonnage, high power and high catch capacity, with large processing plants for catches into animal feed, etc. - is responsible for the overexploitation of resources, to the extent and where it occurs, and for the pollution of the seas. and while neither the commission's proposal nor the report stress the tragic consequences of the action of capital and do not impose on it the enormous responsibilities that fall upon it, the report calls, in essence, for the freezing of fishing activities, the establishment of 'biological resting areas' and measures that will lead to the further development of fish farming, about which the report itself raises questions elsewhere. the generalization of such a policy will bring whole regions of the eu which depend on fishing to a standstill and will harm small and medium-sized fisheries undertakings which are not responsible for industrial pollution or over-exploitation of resources. at the same time, the report, by means of incentives to rehabilitate the fishing fleet and to help fish farming, grants a satisfaction to big business, merely asking it to reorient its activities. for these reasons, we cannot approve the report. however, since some of the points and observations in it are correct, we will not reject it either.

madam president, in deference to your international linguistic approach, commissioner, i will refrain from speaking in my national language and use the lingua franca of our epoch – english. i pay tribute to your valiant rendering, not only of the english language – in which you have no problem – but also of the specialised terminology of the financial markets, which probably caused you more throatache than the language itself. you paid tribute to the modernising efforts of the financial services programme. indeed it is a remarkable programme, which has to succeed if we are going to have a unified and integrated capital market in europe – a great competitive advantage for an economy. however, the word "modernise" can be such a terrible weasel word at the same time. many things can be cloaked in modernism. in this particular case there is an upside, insofar as the integration of capital markets is helped; at the same time, however, the increasing leverage of financial institutions, inter alia, has already given rise to various risks will do so in the future. there have to be ways of strengthening control of those risk-management models which banks will use in order to assume control. such models are very costly and hard to control. when i say "control" i do not mean control in the old-fashioned bureaucratic sense. new methods have to be invented for checking on these models so that they perform in a way that helps the banks perhaps to leverage more while at the same time keeping them in check. if they are not kept in check the risks could be very substantially increased. the other risk in this area is that we create a risk-management divide where big banks and big financial institutions will have all the means at their disposal to engage in modern risk management. meanwhile, small banks will become more and more marginalised in this respect and be increasingly burdened with costs which the bigger institutions have managed to shrug off. if indeed a way out is to be sought through partial application to small banks, this may exacerbate the lack of transparency in the whole area. the recent enron debacle offers a sobering lesson in what a lack of transparency could provoke. therefore our advice would be to temper a little the enthusiasm for surrendering control to the markets, while building up the official control side a bit more and applying it in a modern way using modern methods. such methods might, in the short term, limit profitability, but if they are to spare us a couple of enron-type disasters in the long run, they will more than compensate for any reduction in short term profit.
in writing. - (el) the report acknowledges the dramatic decline in the number of seafarers in the european union and the shortage of well-trained staff and officers. in fact, if we consider that today there are 120 000 seafarers in the eu, whereas in 1978 there were 320 000, we can speak of a clear destruction of the maritime workforce of the eu member states. the report attributes this to secondary causes, avoiding the root causes of the problem. the cause of this destruction is the eu's own maritime policy, which is based on the famous competitiveness of the shipping companies' fleets for the benefit of shipowners' capital and which has disastrous consequences for seafarers' families. in the name of this competitiveness, the eu's common maritime policy has, since the mid-1980s, recognised flags of convenience, which has led to radical changes to the detriment of seafarers and huge gains for big business. on the basis of existing legislation and eu proposals, shipowners' capital exploits the labour of seafarers from third countries as well as from eu member states where wages and social benefits are lower, thus calling into question the basic acquis of workers (collective agreements, seven-hour working days, continuous work and rest periods during the year, etc.). the devaluation of seafarers' training and further training and the disappearance of important previously existing know-how are the result of the eu's own policy, which has encouraged the transformation of diplomas into certificates of suitability to practice the seafarer's trade and the dissociation between the diploma and the profession. the report makes no mention of these enormous problems, it repeats generalizations or, worse, proposes to implement the famous programmes of devalued, fragmented and adapted training for shipowners, and to link training establishments to enterprises. such assessments and proposals are objectively designed, on the one hand, to appease public opinion in relation to the enormous social problem of unemployment, accidents at sea (which sailors call maritime crimes) and ecological disasters, and, on the other hand, to mask the responsibilities of shipowners and their political representatives. in our view, the expansion of the struggle of seafarers at national and international level, their joint action with seafarers in third countries to overturn these policies on the basis of the principle of 'equal work, equal pay', the hiring of seafarers with group insurance rights, collective labour agreements and compulsory public insurance and, finally, the enhancement of the training and further training of seafarers (in particular by granting university status to public maritime schools) can contribute effectively to combating the problems of employment and professional enhancement of seafarers' professions. such a policy can cover the needs of seafarers' families, the safety of human life at sea and the protection of the environment. for the reasons stated above, the members of the communist party of greece will vote against the report.

- mr. president, on friday, february 8th, the 19th winter olympics will begin in salt lake city, usa. as is well known, the olympic games, through the joint efforts of athletes from all countries of the world, aim to promote world peace, friendship, understanding, solidarity, dialogue, and the peaceful coexistence of peoples and cultures. in this spirit, the olympic truce was born in the eighth century b.c.e. and became a fundamental principle of the olympic games. during the games, all hostilities ceased. the guns are down. mr president, since the idea of a ceasefire, that is to say, of world peace, coincides with the ideas and values that have been repeatedly proclaimed by our parliament, i would ask you to send a letter to the president of the olympic committee, mr rogge, and to the secretary-general of the united nations, mr koffi annan, confirming that the european parliament supports the idea of a ceasefire and inviting those involved in conflicts to lay down their arms and to devote the next few weeks, during the olympic games, to resolving their differences by peaceful means.
- mr president, as has been said, the commission's report on the introduction of minimum provisions on the objective nature of the offences and penalties applicable to drug trafficking is a small, cowardly step towards solving the enormous problem of the illegal trafficking of psychoactive substances. the central proposal, which concerns the determination of the penalty, unfortunately refers to the maximum, which should not be less than five years, which leaves the judge free to decide on any penalty up to several years. this is far from the spirit of harmonization, since almost all national laws provide for higher penalties. i should like to stress, however, mr president, that with the amendments tabled - and which i hope will be voted for by the majority - we are able to have some secondary benefits in this decision. first, they make it clear that the addict should not be treated as an offender but as a patient, and that the act of using should not be a criminal offence. secondly, it is proposed that the proceeds from the confiscation of the assets of the major traffickers be used for preventive and curative programmes and, in conclusion, i would like to say that i hope that in future the commission's proposal will be more ambitious, setting clear and harmonised minimum, rather than maximum, penalties for the major traffickers, so as to ensure an area of security and justice within the european union.
the second report (α5-0016/2002) by mr katiforis, on behalf of the committee on economic and monetary affairs, on the proposal for a council directive amending directive 92/79/eec, directive 92/80/eec and directive 95/59/ec as regards the structure and rates of excise duties on manufactured tobacco is on the agenda.
the rapporteur. - mr commissioner, mr council representative, ladies and gentlemen, the treaty states in article 93 that efforts must be made to harmonize specific taxes, that parliament has an advisory role in this process and that the purpose of the treaty is to advance the harmonization of taxation, provided that harmonization is necessary for the establishment and functioning of the internal market within the time limit set by article 14. the commission's first proposal was to serve the aims of article 93, which of course we can only agree with, since they come directly from the treaty. the council has insisted on the urgent need to formulate our opinion, since parliament's role is advisory in this matter. the commission has also put its own pressure on parliament, pointing out that the council has reached unanimous political agreement on the subject, so we have no choice but to accept what has been done in the commission and in the council and to sign it. this approach to parliament has been unfortunate in my view, particularly on the part of the commission, because it amounts to asking parliament to simply sign off on a decision without contributing in any way to its formulation. we are not here to sign open cheques, commissioner. i would also like to say to the council that i hope that its haste to ask for an urgent procedure is not simply to meet a formal condition, but that it will take parliament's opinion seriously, which we are submitting to it, i assure you, after much thought. mr president, parliament has already rejected by a large majority a previous commission proposal. the essence of that proposal was to impose a fixed minimum amount of eur 70 per 1000 cigarettes, applied in parallel to the proportional minimum tax of 57% calculated on the most popular brand on the market. i do not want to go into the technical details of calculating this tax here. parliament rejected the proposal because it accepted the following arguments: the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the minimum fixed amount is not exceeded. indeed, if a small tobacco company manages to offer the most popular brand in its market, lowering its price, it will immediately be faced with a heavy flat tax unrelated to its production costs, which puts it at a disadvantage to the big tobacco companies. secondly, the commission proposal has had a direct effect on the rise in prices in the southern countries, that is to say where per capita income is lower than in the northern countries, in the central zone of the union. to the extent that tax harmonization is achieved, it has been done upwards, by increasing prices. parliament had indeed called for such harmonization in the past, but its latest vote showed that the new parliament resulting from the elections has a different opinion, and moreover the rapporteurs of 1996 and 1999 voted, commissioner, against the commission's proposal. i think parliament is convinced that the continuous appreciation of cigarettes does not achieve the objective, which i share, of reducing smoking, it only reduces legal smoking but on the contrary encourages the smuggling of cigarettes, which has reached alarming proportions in some countries. in addition, the commission's proposal would have led to price increases in the applicant countries reaching the unrealistic level of 200 to 400%. the old commission proposal, which is essentially the basis for the political agreement in the council, therefore does not fulfil the purpose of article 93, because instead of strengthening the internal market it tends to disrupt it. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the proposal is as follows: the council may adopt the commission's initial ideas. parliament will no longer oppose this. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competent authority' to cover the whole of the european union. this option will be based on the combination of a minimum specific tax of 57% and a minimum value added tax of 15%, so that, after applying the relevant formula, the overall tax rate is harmonised at 71%. it is therefore proposed to introduce two taxation methods and to leave the choice to the member state concerned. the arguments in favour of this solution are as follows: the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. secondly, it promotes price harmonization, since there was already price convergence at the minimum tax rate of 57%, whereas the factor causing the fluctuations was value added tax. the commission's proposal is not a new one, but it is a new one, and it is a new proposal, and it is a new proposal. thirdly, it enables member states to protect their smes from the distortions of competition caused by the imposition of a flat-rate tax. all these measures, because they do not weigh up the price increases of cigarettes, discourage smuggling. i think that the council must show courage and imagination, admit that it will have two tax versions and leave the final decision to the member states. i am sure that the new proposal, which i hope my colleagues will adopt, has so many advantages that it will prevail through the choice of member states and soon there will be nothing left but a system of harmonized taxation. the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat tobacco smoking, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for his report. i do not think that this is achieved by continuing to raise prices. cigarettes are already very expensive in europe and smoking does not seem to be getting any smaller. i am therefore opposed to the amendments tabled by my colleagues who want to support the anti-smoking campaign by way of price increases. i do not think that the price mechanism can achieve everything. it can achieve a lot, but it cannot achieve everything, and that is why it is opposed to these proposals. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
- mr president, there is no doubt that smoking is a serious health hazard. it is hypocritical, however, for the commission to claim that it is contributing to the protection of health by means of additional taxation of tobacco products, a product of wide consumption, when it is leaving the consumer unprotected from highly dangerous products in the food chain, imported tobacco products of dubious quality, and pushing for the full commercialization of health. it is certain that with the increase in taxation, and therefore in the price of consumption, we shall not have a reduction in smoking. this would not have happened until now. what will happen with the tax increase is that lower income consumers will be forced to resort to smuggling cheaper cigarettes from third countries, which are probably more harmful to health. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament on the subject of the protection of the environment. the proposed measures will increase the cost of living for the consumer, whose income is constantly being reduced by the european union's policy. we are opposed to the identification of the campaign against smoking with the campaign against tobacco growing in the countries of the european union. the first is in favour of consumer health, while the second is against tobacco growers and processors in the southern european union and in favour of big growers and big manufacturers, mainly in the usa. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the result will be the disappearance of tobacco growing in the european union, the complete domination of imported cigarettes. for these reasons, mr president, we are opposed to any additional special tax because, as we have shown above, it not only does not work to protect public health but rather does the opposite. finally, we would propose that the proceeds from the tobacco tax should go to anti-smoking courses and prevention measures, as well as to tobacco cessation and treatment centres.
commission. – mr president, the new report brings the positions of the commission and parliament closer to each other and i welcome the efforts made by mr katiforis in that respect. his report now proposes to approve the commission proposal subject to two amendments. the first amendment concerns the taxation of cigarettes and offers member states a choice between two taxation systems. the commission is, however, of the opinion that this will lead to less rather than more harmonisation of tobacco taxation. the option proposed would not oblige any member state to change its level of taxation and would even make it possible for a number of member states, including member states applying relatively low tax levels, to lower their rates. this would be in clear contradiction to health policy objectives, as has been pointed out by mrs honeyball this morning. for this reason, the commission cannot accept this first amendment. moreover, it feels that one of the reasons why it is proposed – in particular the need to conserve the balance between specific and percentage taxation – is already taken into account in its proposal. as i explained previously, member states affected by the introduction of the fixed amount in euro retain the flexibility they enjoy at present to determine the proportion of the specific and ad valorem components of cigarette taxation. to justify the amendment, the report also points to the situation of candidate countries, for which the minimum duty level should be an achievable objective. but offering them a choice between two taxation systems after accession would leave us with a wide gap between their tax and price levels and the tax and price levels of the present member states, resulting in substantial revenue loss for present member states. the commission is aware that achieving the amounts in euro will require a significant effort from most candidate countries and has therefore proposed to the council that limited transitional periods should be granted to candidate countries to reach the minimum rate of taxation. the second amendment rejects the change in the definition of cigars and cigarillos. however, the commission remains of the view that products having the characteristics of cigarettes should also be taxed as cigarettes. that change was requested by a number of member states and has the unanimous support of all the others. it has also received substantial support from the cigars and cigarillo industry. i would also like to comment upon the two other amendments adopted in the committee on economic and monetary affairs. the first one suggests adding a new recital, calling upon the commission to submit proposals to eliminate fraud and smuggling. the commission shares the view that rate harmonisation in itself will not eliminate fraud and also that other measures are required to combat fraud and smuggling. however, this does not need to be re-emphasised. a number of initiatives to combat fraud in the sector of excise products, have already been launched following the 1996 report of a high level group on fraud in the alcohol and tobacco sector. the most recent example is the commission proposal of 19 october 2001 for a decision of the european parliament and the council on computerising the movement and surveillance of excise products. it will be more appropriate to discuss the issue of fraud, which is a general issue relevant to all excise products, within the context of this proposal. the second amendment concerns the upper limit of the euro amount proposed by the commission and suggests lowering the amount from eur 100 to eur 85. the motivation of the amendment is that eur 85 is easier to achieve than eur 100 for the applicant countries, which today apply excise duty levels between eur 6 and eur 24. i would point out that this upper limit is not relevant to applicant countries. it is only relevant for member states already applying a very high amount of duty but which, in spite of this, do not meet the 57% minimum. in conclusion, i can say that the commission sees this report as a step forward in comparison with the first report, as it endorses the need for enhanced approximation of rates of excise duty.

the commission is not prepared to accept the amendments tabled by the committee on budgets. the commission cannot confirm the information that the bulgarian government has announced that it does not kowachev. indeed, in the current public debate in bulgaria on the closure of the kosloduy nuclear power plant, namely units 1 to 4, there have been differing opinions within political circles. according to the information available to the commission, in a statement of 10 january 2002 on the early closure of the four reactors at kosloduy, the bulgarian minister of energy indicated the years 2008 and 2010 as the closure dates for units 3 and 4 respectively. the bulgarian energy minister's statement did not concern blocks 1 and 2. the fact is that in the understanding i reached with the bulgarian government in 1999, we agreed that the first and second units would be decommissioned in 2002, that is, that year, that we would agree with the bulgarian government to decommission units 3 and 4 in connection with the review of the country's energy strategy and that this decision should be taken before the original date. the commission has clarified that it understands that the closure of 3 and 4 should take place no later than 2006. but i must make it clear that bulgaria's formal decision on this issue is still pending. this means that we cannot, of course, conclude the negotiations with bulgaria on the energy chapter unless we have a binding date for the decommissioning of blocks 3 and 4. the agreement i reached with the bulgarian government at the time is part of the european union's common negotiating position on the energy chapter concerning kosloduy. it is therefore clear that this chapter cannot be closed unless the content of this agreement is also fulfilled. the commission has already made it clear that the closure of units 1 to 4 of the kosloduy nuclear power plant is an absolutely indispensable condition for the conclusion of accession negotiations with the republic of bulgaria. work has begun on the decommissioning of units 1 and 2 and the necessary administrative and technical conditions have been created. an international fund is available to finance these tasks, so i assume that everything will go according to plan. i do not share the concern expressed by some that the closure of units 1 to 4 will lead to significant economic problems, in particular to significant competition problems. the commission calculates that bulgaria has a considerable excess capacity in energy production and, with a new and modern nuclear power plant under construction, the loss suffered by bulgaria is being compensated and the problems are, in our view, completely manageable. in conclusion, we have not the slightest indication that bulgaria is trying to evade the commitments it has made. and i would like to emphasize that in my recent meeting with the bulgarian prime minister he very explicitly confirmed to me that his government is committed to the commitments made by the previous government.

in writing. - (el) as the communist party of greece denounces on every occasion, the repressive mechanism and the police regime that are being put in place by the european union constitute the response of capitalist society to the growing movement of protest and demand that its anti-social policy in the service of the interests of big capital provokes. the eu leaders know that, despite their efforts, they cannot deceive the people by hiding the substance of their policies under a veil of populism. this process was developing slowly but steadily until 9/11. the abominable events of that day served as a pretext to accelerate the implementation of political decisions that had been taken long before. the authors and instigators of the terrorist attacks have given a great gift to the american and european imperialists. we have thus, for the second time, examined the improved proposals for council framework decisions on the fight against terrorism and the european arrest warrant, which are the heavy artillery of the repression mechanism. nothing essential has changed in the texts of the decisions, apart from some formal improvements which were imposed under pressure from the reaction, and the addition of the statement that 'the european union is founded on the universal principles of human dignity, freedom, equality and solidarity, respect for human rights; it shall operate on the basis of the principle of democracy and the rule of law.' the hypocrisy of this declaration is that the framework decisions seriously undermine democracy and collective and individual social rights. under article 1, anyone who is fighting for fundamental political and social change can easily be considered a terrorist. forms of mobilisation such as symbolic occupations, or the consequences of mobilisations such as disruptions in the supply of electricity or water when the sectors concerned strike, can easily be considered terrorist crimes if they result in "considerable economic losses". the same applies, of course, to the farmers' mobilizations blocking the roads. the extension of responsibility to organisations of which 'terrorists' are members creates a convenient mechanism for prosecuting troublesome organisations or parties, but also for inciting provocations. the only ones above suspicion are the masters of the eu and the us. they are not liable to prosecution under their own law, which considers acts of terrorism to be those intended to cause serious damage to the country, when committed with the aim of: (i) seriously threatening a population; or (ii) unreasonably forcing public authorities or an international body to act or to refrain from acting; or (iii) seriously destabilising or destroying the fundamental political, constitutional, economic and social structures of a country or an international body. what have the european and american imperialists done less, done more in yugoslavia, iraq, afghanistan, chile, greece and dozens of other countries? the people of the world know who the terrorists are. they also know that violence and repression cannot reverse the course of human history. they will continue their struggle until all anti-terrorist laws are effectively abolished. for the reasons i have just mentioned, we as members of the communist party of greece voted against the report.
in writing. - (el) the report, as regards the basic technical principles of monitoring bse, poses the problems in a positive way. we support the proposals concerning the adoption of the precautionary the rapporteur. - mr president, in this proposal for a directive we are talking about extending restrictions already in force on the speed of certain public transport vehicles to certain other categories of vehicles, passengers and lorries, and we mean public transport vehicles smaller than those to which restrictions have been applied up to now. we're talking about the m2 and m3 categories, which are passenger cars, and the n2 categories, which are trucks. the european commission's proposal provides for a speed limit for passengers of 100 km and for lorries of 90 km, with special mechanisms. i am in agreement with the commission's proposal, mr president. in the netherlands, a study has been carried out - and the european commission has presented it to us - which shows that we have a profit of €40 million a year in transport and a social benefit of €89 million. in europe, if we do the reductions, we have a benefit of about 3 billion euros. but i do not support this study, because many may doubt its accuracy. i want to be a little bit of common sense. and common sense tells us that when we have this speed limit we will have a gain, firstly in road safety, because we will have fewer accidents, secondly we will have a gain in the field of environmental pollution and, thirdly, we will have a guarantee of fair competition between businesses, because we will have the same limit everywhere, all over europe, while at the same time companies will not be able to force their drivers to drive too fast and thus, in the name of profit, to risk human lives. the amendments i tabled do not therefore make much difference to the content of the european commission's proposal. i would like to refer to two amendments which were accepted in the debate we had in our committee. the first concerns an exemption which we propose to be made for five years from the entry into force of the directive, in categories m2 and n2 for those vehicles weighing more than 3.5 tonnes and less than 7.5 tonnes. we propose that the directive should apply five years after its entry into force and, secondly, we ask the european commission to carry out a study on the so-called intelligent speed adjustment systems 18 months after the directive enters into force, so as to see whether, by applying these systems, we can vary the speeds according to the circumstances, and of course we can have even lower speeds in urban areas. we have tabled an amendment as the european people's party on category n2, which includes small lorries, where we ask for the speed limit to be increased from 90 km to 110 km because they are a different type, they are smaller cars and i think we can be more flexible here. i am not saying that the commission should not be involved in this. they have ideological, political reasons and they do not think that the european commission, the european union should intervene in this matter. i want to thank them, i understand their reasons and, although i disagree with them, i respect their position. but i would ask them, since there are some amendments which make the content of the original commission proposal more flexible and are closer to their position, to consider these amendments constructively, and if they can vote for them, since my prediction is that, whatever their own position, this directive will eventually become european legislation.

in writing. - (el) the galileo programme concerns the construction and installation of a satellite navigation system which will enable its users to determine their position on the earth with high precision. in addition to the increased accuracy it will provide compared to the us global positioning system (gps), which has been in operation for a few years, the essential feature of galileo is that it was designed to be placed under political control, not military, as is the case with gps. at the same time, it is the first space operation at european union level, and its successful installation and operation will, apart from obviously contributing to the improvement of navigation, give a major boost to space science and technology. the commission communication we have studied does not concern the galileo system itself, but the creation of a joint venture between the public and private sectors which will bring together the capital necessary for its development. the report by mr glante proposes important improvements to the commission's proposal. despite this, the european parliament approved by a large majority, and under the responsibility of the group of the european people's party and the co-responsibility of the group of the european socialist party, two amendments which seek to militarize the system, by explicitly referring to the possibility of using it under the pretext of 'peacekeeping operations'. to the extent that this logic is accepted by the council and the commission, the non-military character of the system is completely destroyed and, of course, the arsenal of the european union for intervening against the peoples is strengthened. it is remarkable that the european parliament approved a similar amendment in the report on the european union's space policy, which i had presented at the previous session on behalf of the committee on industry, foreign trade, research and energy. the coincidence of these two events is certainly not accidental but, as we also pointed out last time, it shows that there is a clear intention to use space for military purposes. these developments show that the world popular movement must intensify its struggle against imperialist aggression and the militarization of space. that is why the members of the european parliament from the greek communist party voted against the report.

the rapporteur. - mr president, commissioner, it is a great honour for me to present the report on the five years of application of the working time directive. working time is, along with wages, one of the leading issues of workers' demands throughout the course of industrial relations. these demands and successes are an offering of the trade union movement, because the arrangements it has achieved have enabled workers to regain their lost personality, to improve their living conditions, to reduce the risks to their biological existence, to participate in political and social life through the liberation of working time. the legal basis of the directive is health and safety provisions, but the other aspects must not be underestimated. this is why this acquis should be safeguarded by the member states as a cornerstone of social cohesion. it also gives me the right to express my regret that a number of member states have not been willing to transpose the directive into national law in time, whereas this should have been regarded as a self-evident obligation. i would also like to comment on the fact that the member states have not provided detailed statements of their national regulations so that the commission can clearly ascertain the extent to which they comply with the provisions of the directive. it is therefore not entirely clear whether and to what extent it applies to all public sector employees. the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for adoption. one of these issues is the issue of doctors' diaries, but it is linked to the more general issue of the assessment of readiness for work as working time. in this sense, it also concerns other categories of workers. the solution given by the eca, according to which when readiness is accompanied by physical presence at the workplace and by alertness of physical and mental forces constitutes normal paid working time, is correct according to the traditional positions of labour law. another issue is the distortions of the right to leave through the use of fixed-term employment contracts. the court of justice's answer that the right to leave is one of the fundamental rights and is also enjoyed by contractors 'prorata tempores' when they are linked to fixed-term contracts of less than one year is also positive in the fight against circumvention. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the minimum working time is not exceeded. it is strange that the regulation which has been in force for decades is still being questioned in its specific meaning. the transposition of the directive into certain countries with collective agreements is a major issue. the subject is of more general interest. there is no doubt that this method of promoting collective autonomy should be encouraged. however, the method must be used in such a way as to produce the same results as its transposition by legislative act. this is not the case at present, either because the sectoral collective agreement is used, rather than a general inter-professional agreement covering all employees, or because there are no collective agreements applicable to all employees. finally, in addition to the arrangements in the context of traditional industrial relations, there is the problem of special arrangements for specific categories of workers with special needs, such as women, mothers, disabled persons, children, etc. these arrangements not only need to be given extra attention because they concern vulnerable groups, but they must also be improved on the basis of experience to date and in order to be integrated into the european union's policy of increasing the participation of women in the labour force and facilitating access to the labour market for people with disabilities. i would like to dedicate the last part of my speech to the fate of traditional arrangements after the emergence of new forms of employment, where both the principle of flexibility and where traditional employment security are being transformed into employability. here bold innovation is needed to ensure the declared lisbon principle of combining flexibility with protection and transforming this principle in relation to the organisation of working time into concrete measures. the replacement of traditional employment security with employability requires a new approach to working time arrangements because new problems arise, such as how parttime work can be converted into fulltime work, what the fate of weekly rest and the prohibition of working on holidays is, to what extent the conversion of wage labour into self-employment requires adjustment of working time arrangements, how in the case of teleworking the application of fixed working time provisions can be controlled, what new balances must be sought between work and family life and finally, of course, how the time of lifelong learning will be regarded as working time. in conclusion, i would like to add that all these issues of modern forms of organisation are rightly highlighted by the european trade unions as matters of immediate priority with a wide-ranging campaign which they want to launch on 14 february on the occasion of today's debate.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the working time regulations we are considering are part of the neoliberal policy which is overturning the industrial relations which the working class has won with its struggles. this policy is a blow to workers' rights and implements flexible forms of employment and working time arrangements, it is a blow to full-time and fixed employment and replaces it with part-time or temporary employment. the aim is, of course, to increase the degree of exploitation of the labour force and to maximise the profits of big capital. the aim is to abolish the fixed daily working time of eight or seven hours and the weekend holiday, while collective agreements are being introduced and fixed-term contracts are being extended. the employee must be available to the employer whenever and for as long as he wishes. the commission has also proposed that the member states should be required to provide for the possibility of a reduction in the number of employees in the sector, in order to ensure that the number of employees in the sector is not reduced. the result of the european union's policies is the spread of underemployment, which in some countries exceeds 30%. we support a different policy, one which extends the rights of workers and fights against capitalist restructuring. we are fighting for the abolition of the legislative framework which requires the regulation of daily working hours and flexible forms of employment. we support full and stable work with seven-hour and five-day work, the extension of social rights and wage increases in line with modern needs.

- (el) mr president, a few days ago the pastrana government, exploiting a hijacking of an airliner, which is in fact nothing but a provocation condemned by the farc and to which they declared themselves to be foreign, broke off negotiations and began to mercilessly bomb the demilitarised zone, thus implementing the us 'colombia' plan, which aims to destroy the farc and impose a military solution to the problem to the detriment of the interests of the colombian people. it is certain that, as the american plan foresees, the deadly bombing will be followed by paramilitary groups, who will come to complete the slaughter. i would therefore like to invite you, mr president, alongside your intervention in favour of the release of the green candidate, to ask the pastrana government to stop the bombing, to release the hundreds of farc cadres held in inhumane conditions, and your three irish compatriots, who are held in the same conditions. i also invite you to ask for the resumption of negotiations with a view to a peaceful settlement of the problems.

- the next item is the recommendation for second reading (a5-0027-2002), on behalf of the committee on industry, foreign trade, research and energy, on the common position of the council with a view to the adoption of a regulation of the european parliament and of the council on the implementation of the '.eu' first level internet domain.
commission. – the adoption of the regulation to make the .eu top level domain a reality is now reaching its final stage. i would like to express my thanks, particularly to the rapporteur, mrs flesch, for her sustained efforts to reach a compromise. i wish also to thank, in particular, mrs mccarthy and mr harbour and also mr carraro. their understanding of some of the complexities raised by this initiative has contributed significantly to the outcome we are discussing today. it is my pleasure to indicate that the commission can accept the compromise amendments from 7 to 12. several of the amendments proposed introduce useful additional clarifications to the provisions of the common position. the commission can support the amendment relating to the examination of the first-come, first-served method amongst the existing options when defining the registration policy and within the boundaries of the regulation's framework. it can also support the amendment introducing the cost-recovery principle in the policy relating to the extra-judicial settlement of conflicts. the most complex issue to address was without doubt the type of committee and procedure to select for this regulation. the commission can accept amendments 7, 8, 9, 10, 11 and 12. with respect to amendment 6 of the recommendation for second reading, as adopted by the industry, external trade, research and energy committee, the commission cannot support the amendment to article 8. however, in the light of parliament's concern over the implementation of the .eu top level domain, the commission wishes to state the following: the commission shares the view of the european parliament that, to be successful, the .eu top level domain should be in place very quickly and should not be burdened by an excessive regulatory framework. an advisory procedure was therefore considered by the commission to be the most appropriate for this regulation. in line with the decision adopted at the stockholm european council in march 2001 to adopt all the necessary measures to ensure that the .eu top level domain is available users, as soon as possible, the commission has resolved, however, to accept the regulatory procedure for article 3(1) and article 5, in order to facilitate the early adoption of this regulation, and with the confidence that this step can further promote the spirit of collaboration needed to achieve the objective set at the stockholm council. the commission stresses that the selection of the registry, to organise administer and manage the .eu top level domain, should take place as early as possible and that all reasonable efforts will be made to ensure a prompt publication of the call for expressions of interest after the entry into force of the regulation. this presupposes that the criteria and procedures for the designation of the registry are completed in due time. the commission is committed to submitting the measures required for the implementation of the .eu top level domain in a timely fashion and shall do its utmost to ensure that the relevant procedures are completed and promptly to allow the .eu registry to become operational as early as possible after the entry into force of the regulation, unless exceptional circumstances intervene. the commission calls on the member states to fully subscribe to this objective and to provide the support required for achieving it.

the commission. - mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i have to say that i am very pleased with the way the commission has been working. the convention is a very important step forward. the convention on the future of europe will, i believe, also determine the face of europe. i am glad that an agreement, an understanding, on financing was reached so quickly after initially misleading speculation, including on the amount of funds needed. it has already been mentioned: the total cost for this year is estimated at eur 10.5 million. the member states and candidate countries are to bear part of the cost, namely the travel expenses of their members. the european institutions, however, bear the bulk of the costs. i would also like to mention the part of parliament that is provided in the framework of infrastructure resources, namely interpretation and translation capacities. an estimate of eur 4 million is still needed for various other expenditure. what are these other various issues? for example, funding for studies that need to be done or perhaps even more translation capacity. the estimate is therefore eur 4 million, and there has been an extremely rapid and consensual agreement on how to raise this eur 4 million, namely eur 400 000 from the council, eur 1 million from the european parliament, eur 2.6 million from the commission, and this is now being fed directly from the supplementary budget into a fund which will then be available to the convention. the interinstitutional agreement which will be signed tomorrow will, i believe, be a jointly agreed solution to the problem, one which will ensure the greatest possible transparency. the three institutions are thus involved both in drawing up the budget for the convention and in any amendment to that budget and in discharge, in such a way that, in particular, discharge requires the prior agreement of all three institutions. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. it is quite clear that this construction is very exceptional and i am glad that parliament has emphasized this in its resolution. it is also in the commission's interest, and i would like to stress this, that this exceptional character be emphasized. the convention is exceptional, and the solution we have found makes it clear above all that it is in the interests of all three institutions that this convention should work successfully. i think that one thing can be said again: contrary to all speculation, we have found a good solution here too very quickly, and it is very good that the interinstitutional agreement can be signed tomorrow along with the decision on the supplementary and amending budget. in any case, we have done a good job with the committee on budgetary control and the committee on budgets in creating the conditions for successful work!

- the next item is the report (a5-0028/2002) by mr gahrton, on behalf of the committee on foreign affairs, human rights, common security and defence policy, on the communication from the commission to the council and the european parliament on the european union's relations with the south caucasus in the framework of the partnership and cooperation agreements.
- mr president, as i begin by congratulating you and wishing you the very best in your presidency, i should like to congratulate mr gahrton on his excellent work and on his ability to combine and articulate in his report the main positions of the various political groups on the various subjects. this has been a painstaking task, but it has resulted in a well-crafted and balanced text, which, thanks to the helpful amendments of my colleagues, gives a realistic and objective account of the situation in the south caucasus, the problems and the outlined prospects and the various aspects and parameters of the relations between the three countries of the region and the european union. based on the generally accepted view that the south caucasus region is of particular importance for europe and noting that the european union has, in various political and economic ways, expressed its willingness to strengthen the process of ensuring progress, political stability and the rule of law in the countries concerned, i believe it appropriate to stress the desirability of further strengthening the role of the european union in formulating a strategy to solve the problems of the region, which should be pursued taking into account the specificities and national and historical sensitivities of the peoples and encouraging the neighbouring states, in particular russia, iran and turkey, to contribute positively to the reconstruction of the south caucasus. in this context, the report rightly calls on turkey, which is in fact linked to the european union by a partnership, to live up to its european ambitions by ending its blockade of armenia and creating a basis for reconciliation with that country, in accordance with the compromise paragraph 15 of the report which i urge my colleagues to vote in favour of. russia has a similarly important role to play as a catalyst for conflict prevention in the south caucasus and for resolving the highly sensitive energy issue, whether the countries of the region are involved as producers or consumers of energy. in conclusion, i would like to stress that the european union's interest in the south caucasus region must be maintained and manifested unabated, by actively supporting the expressed desire of the countries concerned to belong to europe and by close cooperation of the european union and its institutions with them in all areas.
the commission is not prepared to accept the amendments. i welcome mr gahrton's report as a timely and useful contribution to our reflections on armenia, georgia and azerbaijan, three countries currently members of the council of europe, which have not reached the level of political and economic development to which they aspire and which we wish them to. the conflicts in nagorno-karabakh, abkhazia and south ossetia continue to pose a threat to the stability and development of the region and its neighbours more than anything else. these conflicts, frozen today, could become new hot spots tomorrow, and in that sense i fully share the view of mr gahrton when he says that the european union must make great efforts to help solve these problems, with a view to promoting the causes of peace and development. we are not starting from scratch, so before i go over some of the key points in this report, i will briefly summarise what our current relations are with this region. the partnership and cooperation agreements we have concluded with each of the three countries provide a unique platform for cooperation in a wide range of areas, from political dialogue, trade and investment, to relations with civil society. implementation of these agreements, which entered into force in 1999, is gathering momentum. we also continue to provide significant levels of assistance to armenia, georgia and azerbaijan. the european union has provided some €1 billion since 1991, when these countries became independent. last year, the european union sent a clear signal to the region that it wants to improve its contribution to conflict prevention, conflict resolution and post-conflict rehabilitation. in february 2001, the first ministerial troika of the union for the region conveyed this message, which was subsequently confirmed in the conclusions of the general affairs council of 26 february. in a joint statement adopted at the cooperation council meetings last october, the three partner countries explicitly welcomed the union's intentions. mr gharton draws our attention to some of the instruments we have used elsewhere and suggests that we apply them in the south caucasus: common strategy, stability pact, special envoy and other issues. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament on the subject. instead of creating new instruments, we should focus on maximising the benefits of existing instruments. let me outline three aspects of our thinking in this respect: firstly, the political will of the participants is, more than anything else, the key, which can produce progress. in the present situation it is essential that we maximise our efforts to support the commitments, particularly through political dialogue. difficult choices will have to be made and a culture of compromise will have to be developed in the south caucasus. secondly, in order for the european union to be able to participate fully in conflict resolution, we need to further intensify our common foreign and security policy and our role in measures that promote conflict resolution. i am referring in particular to the osce minsk group for nagorno-karabakh, the friends of the secretary-general of abkhazia and the joint monitoring committee on south ossetia. we have made progress recently; the european union has been involved in the work of the joint monitoring committee on south ossetia since last year, but much remains to be done to make the union's voice heard in certain mediation environments. thirdly, we must ensure that the south caucasus is definitely on the agenda of our relations with the main players in the region, such as russia, turkey, iran and the united states, and as some of the honourable members have said when they have intervened, also in our own interest, inter alia for reasons relating to energy supplies. in conclusion, there is no single and easy answer to the question of how to improve the union's relations with the south caucasus. the gahrton report rightly reminds us that we must work patiently along various parallel lines, probably over a long period of time, within the limits of their resources and building on their achievements of the past year. the commission wishes to explore new ways of promoting peace and development in the south caucasus. i would remind you that the construction of europe will have to be limited at some point, and i would remind you that in terms of geographical proximity and historical ties, we have enormous historical ties and geographical proximity with countries such as morocco, algeria and the mediterranean.

- mr president, the socrates programme is not just a system of educational change. it is a tool for intervening in the consciousness of young people, but also a mechanism for imposing a specific educational model. the essential problem of education, which is the universal education of young people, is left out of the programme's activities. on the contrary, its actions are aimed at transforming the consciousness of pupils, students and teachers, so as to consolidate the european one-way street and facilitate the submission of young people to the policy of the european union, the rules of the market economy and the so-called new working conditions. the european education area conceals within itself the convergence of education systems in the direction of their adaptation to the needs of monopoly capital, which does not require the overall upgrading of education. even educational exchanges and the course reservation system act as a means of pressure in this direction. the real upgrading of education systems requires the rejection of european union interventions. it is, in the final analysis, a matter for the popular movement.

in writing. - (el) the attempt to ban the hadep party in turkey and the trial against it, which will open tomorrow in ankara, are just one example of the violation of basic democratic rights in this of turkey that if it does not change its name, it will be banned. some of its members were later arrested in the course of their political activity and its offices were the target of attacks. there prisoners themselves and among their family members and companions who assist them. it should be recalled that the hunger strike to the death, which began on 20 october 2000, is still ongoing and has already caused dozens of deaths. we ask the european parliament to join the voice of the democrats in turkey, who are demanding the free and unhindered functioning of political parties, the of type f or accept the proposal of the turkish bar association and other social organisations to unify three cells, according to the well-known principle of 'three doors, three locks'. it is clear obstacles to the flourishing of the kurdish people's culture and language, why there are more than 10 000 political prisoners in prisons, held in conditions of the most miserable conditions known to man, why the functioning of political parties opposed to the policies of the turkish regime is banned or threatened with a ban, why fighters for democracy are arrested and murdered every day, and why - mr president, i will refer to the same subject raised by mr wurtz, namely the new american nuclear weapons development programme, to emphasize that this is not simply a new escalation of nuclear arms competition, with incalculable consequences for humanity. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the nuclear industry is not left behind, and that the nuclear industry is not left behind. the threat of nuclear attack also exists even in countries that do not possess nuclear weapons themselves, and the pentagon report even suggests that conventional weapons systems should be given nuclear capability. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. if the people of the world do not stop them in time, it is very possible that our planet will cease to exist.
the commission is aware of the difficulties facing the community in the field of the internal market. i shall try to make some general comments on the various contributions made today, because unfortunately we cannot go into more detail in the time available. firstly, i think we can all agree that the current model in which we operate is leading to a drift towards totally unacceptable unilateral models. we must insist on this point. globalisation has rules which must be respected; otherwise, any globalisation process is bound to fail, and unilateral movements will have serious problems in promoting the advantages of globalisation. secondly, the process of globalisation - and i think we can all agree on this - has brought benefits to economic development; the problem is that it also has drawbacks, and therefore our job is not - or should not be - to attack globalisation, but a particular model of globalisation or the negative aspects of a particular model of globalisation. when the commission analysed these issues - which is why i have presented all three reports together - it came to the conclusion that the concept of globalisation must include at least the elements referring to the existence of an efficient international financial system, development aid systems and elements linked to the environment and sustainable development. after hearing your comments, i will refer to each of these issues: firstly, development aid. we all agree that the fight against poverty is a fundamental element of development aid and that the problem of development aid is not only a problem of quantity but also of quality. in this sense, i am absolutely in favour of the idea of improving governance in some of these countries, which is a key element for a better functioning of such development aid. however, improving the quality of development aid should not make us forget the quantity of aid, which we must recognize is still insufficient. it is true that we may run the risk of not accepting the commission's relatively pragmatic proposal to achieve at least 0.33% for the countries of the union in 2006. in any event, other third countries do not accept this proposal. we forget a fundamental issue in the whole process of future development: capital investment. direct investment is playing a fundamental role in the whole development process, and elements such as governance play a key and fundamental role in this, as do - as some of you have commented - all the elements linked to the best technology transfers and any other element that allows the most appropriate development of these countries. i would like to make a very specific comment on debt: it is true that the european union has made a fundamental effort in the cancellation of the acp countries' debts. the union's contribution is now over eur 1 billion and the initial target is therefore well on track. is this enough? possibly not. we probably have to think of more ambitious elements. in our report on globalisation we look at possible systems for obtaining more resources to cope with development processes. i certainly share the view of those who have said that solutions should not be simplistic. unfortunately, we have not found a single solution that will allow us to solve all the problems. the commission believes that we must move forward together and at the same time on the issues of globalisation, development aid and the environment, without forgetting a fundamental framework: the outcome of the doha commitments. trade plays a fundamental role, as does the transfer of investment, in development aid, and it needs to be done more equitably in the future. for the negotiations that follow from doha, it is not only essential that we defend our views as the european union, but also that the developing countries defend theirs. that is why, in addition to the 'everything but arms' initiative, the commission has proposed technical assistance initiatives for these countries, so that they can better defend their interests and, in the end, achieve a much fairer system. all these elements, taken together, will allow us to improve, but in a few years' time we will still be talking about the necessary challenges of globalisation. we must set in motion a path that will enable us to achieve a more just society, even if this is a long-term objective.

the commission has not yet taken a decision on the application of the principle of equal treatment to the various categories of goods and services. the single market does not exist and i do not foresee it in the near future. the competitive functioning of the internal market in electricity and gas is a key prerequisite for achieving the lisbon objectives of a dynamic and competitive european union with full employment. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's social policy is not distorted by the introduction of new forms of taxation, such as the one proposed by the commission in its white paper on the internal market. instead of applying prices which do not reflect the real cost of production and which impede competition, social policy is better pursued by other means, e.g. by reducing taxes. finally, any delay in the opening of markets is a deterrent to private enterprises and foreign investment in the energy sector and the european union in general, and therefore does not increase employment, which is supposed to be the main objective.
- mr president, the amendments we are considering are aimed at liberalizing the market generally, even in distribution, even to the last customer. the aim is to develop further business activities in a sector which, until now, was under state control and inaccessible to capital. the case of transport systems, which are essentially under the management of public bodies, which have to cover only the costs, while the private users will shape prices in accordance with the maximum possible profit, is characteristic of the favour to capital. we also mention that in our country the state electricity company is forced to buy electricity at exorbitant prices from private individuals, just to get the latter into the market. liberalisation ignores social needs, such as the provision of energy to islands and other hard-to-reach areas, but also causes obvious difficulties in long-term planning. the argument that prices are falling is not valid, as the fully liberalised markets of germany and the united kingdom offer significantly higher prices than the partially liberalised greek market. it is also very serious that the amendments increase the involvement of the european commission. in conclusion, the only ones who will benefit from the new arrangements are the large monopolies, which will penetrate the markets even further with the help of the regulation on cross-border exchanges.

signed by 100 prominent figures, denouncing the catholic church for its unacceptable interference and attempt to control the reproductive functions of women.
