the commission is therefore proposing that the council and parliament adopt a regulation on the introduction of legal measures to combat unfair competition by airlines from third countries which benefit from state aid. the reality is that we have all seen over the past few months how, due to various circumstances, airlines from third countries have received large public subsidies which have been used in many cases to lower fares and to make unfair competition on routes where they were competing with airlines from countries of the european union. this has created a legal vacuum which has put our companies at a disadvantage vis-à-vis those of third countries. in order to have the instruments to defend and support our air transport sector, which is absolutely strategic for the european union, we are going to propose and today we have adopted the proposal for a regulation on predatory practices in the framework of air transport services. in the field of maritime transport there are already regulations to combat tariffs which create unfair competition and in other areas, such as aid granted in the manufactured goods sector, we have other instruments. all this, ladies and gentlemen, for one very simple reason: because the air services sector is not covered by the multilateral agreements of the world trade organization. the aim is to establish compensatory measures to avoid the advantage that the use of such state aid or non-commercial advantages would give to companies from these third countries to the detriment of our companies. i think it is a balanced regulation, which sets out - as you will see when you look at it - a definition of what the principles are, what it means to guarantee fair competition, what is meant by unfair pricing practices and establishes a procedure which ensures that there will be no overcompensation or unfair treatment under any circumstances. in this connection, either by means of a complaint or merely on its own initiative, it is proposed that the commission should open an investigation to determine whether or not there is a situation of unfairness in the tariffs charged by third-party companies, and, depending on that situation, either there is an agreement with the country and the companies concerned or, if not, measures can be taken, both provisional and definitive, for 6 months, until there is a change on the part of the third countries and the companies concerned. i think it is absolutely necessary if we are to ensure that we are really going to prevent unfair competition and that our airline industry is not affected by state aid or non-commercial advantages.
- mr president, i think it is right that the commission is moving in this direction because we must have some legal instruments as the european union to deal with the specific issue of unfair competition from third countries. the commission's proposal will certainly be discussed in our own transport committee of the european parliament and we will see what the details are. but now i would like to ask you three very brief questions. firstly, do you have any evidence or even complaints from various european companies that there is unfair competition from third countries, america, switzerland or other countries that we have occasionally read in the press? secondly, you mentioned measures that the european union is going to take in the event of such events. what kind of measures will be taken and how do they relate to the existing transatlantic agreements on which the advocate general of the european court of justice has already taken a position? what is the relationship between bilateral agreements and the european commission's multilateral measures? thirdly, a question which is not directly relevant to the subject but which is very topical and which you can answer if you wish. what about the insurance cover for european airlines? coverage ends at the end of march. what is the position of the european commission?
commission. – mr president, an infringement procedure against greece was opened in 1996 regarding the failure of the greek authorities to transpose directive 94/62 on packaging and packaging waste international legislation. in july 1998 the commission received notification from the greek authorities on a draft law which would transpose directive 94/62. however, this draft law was not immediately adopted into greek legislation and so transposition did not actually occur in 1998. on 13 april 2000 the european court of justice condemned greece for non-transposition of directive 94/62. on 2 may 2000 the commission requested information from the greek authorities regarding the measures taken to comply with the court ruling, but no information was provided. the procedure stemming from article 228 was initiated against greece on 8 november 2000 and a reasoned opinion was sent to greece on 10 april 2001. on 2 august 2001 greece adopted law 2939/2001, thereby transposing directive no 94/62 into greek national legislation. the commission received notification of this law from the greek authorities on 26 september 2001. this law has satisfactorily incorporated the observations of the commission concerning the notification text transmitted to the commission in 1998 and the procedure against greece under article 228 regarding transposition of directive 94/62 was therefore closed on 20 december 2001.
- commissioner, thank you very much for the information, which was complete and full of dates and figures. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the directive is implemented. i say this because it is a very bad coincidence, yesterday and the day before yesterday we had heavy rains in athens, together with a strike of street cleaners, and the huge volume of waste, which is the aim of this directive, clogged all the sewers and flooded the city. can you tell me what you're planning beyond the formal introduction of the bill? because i think there are deadlines. could you tell me what your estimate is? when will we start to have waste reduction and other types of policy, recycling, reuse, etc.?
the commission is not prepared to accept the amendments tabled by the committee on budgets. the quota buyback programme in the tobacco sector was introduced to facilitate the transition of producers who want to leave the sector voluntarily. the repurchase price of certain tobacco varieties, whose market opportunities are deteriorating, was then significantly increased in order to provide producers with an incentive to abandon their cultivation. however, when buying the quotas from the buyback programme, the other tobacco growers who want to buy quotas have priority. we can only buy back the quantities that the other tobacco farmers do not buy. for the next three years, the commission has put forward a proposal for a council regulation to fix the premiums and quotas until 2004. the commission proposal provides for a reorganization of the community tobacco fund to enable the member states to finance studies on the possibilities of switching tobacco production to other crops outside the tobacco sector and on the first experience of this switch. in this proposal, the commission also announced a further proposal for the first half of 2003, which will be based on the guidelines of the commission's communication of 16 may of the previous year on the european union's strategy for sustainable development. this requires a clear understanding of the possible alternatives and the socioeconomic consequences of phasing out tobacco promotion. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's tobacco industry is not left behind.

the council. - mr president, i would like to thank all the members of this house who have spoken. i've listened to you very carefully. unfortunately, i do not have time to answer them individually, as i would like to do. i believe that this was an extremely useful exchange of ideas and impressions for all and that it confirms the full cooperation between the different institutions of the european union and, in particular, between the council, parliament and the commission. but i would like to make some general comments which may be relevant. i believe that there is absolutely majority support, of course, practically without any gaps, for this great historic project of enlargement. and, moreover, the clear awareness that we must meet the timetables we have set so that, in the next elections to the european parliament in 2004, many of the countries that are candidates today can join and participate. there has been a reference to the historical component of enlargement and it is absolutely true. it is a political project, but it is also a historical project, aimed at the final reunification of europe after the division marked by the cold war and the balance of terror, after the division of europe into blocs. but it is true that with this enlargement we are probably not in a position to say that we are completing the construction of europe from a geographical point of view. we are all talking about up to 10 countries - and i will refer to that now - to conclude the negotiations at the end of 2002. we are working on it; there are still many difficulties and many obstacles; some members of parliament have referred to the situation in bulgaria, romania, turkey, the balkans and ukraine. i mean that many times, when we are intensifying our criticism of the current process of european integration, we forget that the european integration and the political project of the union itself are the clearest example of a success story which began with six and has now reached fifteen; in a very short time we may be twenty-five, then twenty-seven, twenty-eight, who knows if in the future there will be many more. nobody wants to join a club that is failing, that is not working well, but everyone wants to join, as in this case, a club that is working well and has a successful outcome. i will take the example of the balkans, both east and west. so i can also speak of bulgaria and romania. i said in my speech - and commissioner verheugen confirmed it - that we need to send very strong, very serious and very positive messages to bulgaria and romania, in order to open all the chapters over the next few months with them and to speed up the negotiations for their future accession as much as possible. for these countries the european perspective is particularly important, as it was for my country at the time. for spain, europe has always been a beacon of freedom, democratic consolidation, social justice, modernisation and economic progress. and for those countries it is the same and they have exactly the same right as we had when we were able to join the european union. and that reflection, only with more time ahead, we can make with regard to the western balkans. what are we offering these countries? a perspective, a horizon of integration, and the need to create a new dimension in the european union. we are offering them the possibility of continuing their democratic consolidation and their internal reforms, because there is no alternative to the prospect and the horizon of european integration. this will be the best antidote to preventing the recurrence of confrontations and the war situation that these countries have experienced relatively recently. the european union is therefore launching, within the framework of the stability pact, the association and stability agreements with a number of these countries. they have different rates, but our clear aim is that all of them, at some point, may aspire to become candidates for membership of the european union. that's the project we're on and that's why it's so important that we get it right now. we must be serious, rigorous, disciplined and realistic. i have listened very carefully to some members of this parliament who have spoken of rethinking the financial perspective and saying that enlargement cannot be free. it will not be free, but - which is quite different - it will be included in the framework of the financial perspective we have agreed. i have also heard some speakers say that we must start thinking about reforms of common policies. of course, but if anyone says today - and i want to be clear and decisive - that these debates, both on the financial perspective and on the reform of common policies, must be mixed up with the accession negotiations, he must know that this would prevent the timetables for enlargement from being met. anyone who wants enlargement and wants us to stick to the timetables we have set should know that we either do this or we will not get it. i think there is a complete agreement between the council and the commission and i certainly hope that there is agreement with parliament. only by reaching a broad consensus will we be able to do what all our citizens are asking us to do. i would like to conclude with one last comment which i also think is relevant. the european union has a clear political will to enlarge. but this political will does not mean that the political decision has already been taken to necessarily close the negotiations with 10 candidates before the end of the year. the political will is essential, but it is not enough, because we must keep up the pace of the negotiations and the applicant countries must demonstrate their administrative, judicial and, in short, their political capacity, not only to transpose the community acquis into their legislation, but also to convince us all that they are capable of complying with it, because otherwise we will be in trouble, both the current members and them. and we're all committed to making this work. i hope that the clear political will of the european union will coincide with the clear political will of the applicant countries to do what they must, so that we can actually make the political decision at the right time to close the negotiations, with 10 countries at first, and in the future with more.
the commission is not prepared to accept the amendments tabled by the committee on budgets. i would like to thank parliament very warmly for the broad support which the presidency of the council and the commission have found in the enlargement process, and i think it is very important that it remains in this commonality and in this cooperation. a few very brief remarks: firstly, i think it is necessary to make it very clear once again that we must not create new conditions for enlargement, although it is desirable to push ahead with certain reforms within the policies and also within the construction of the european union. i understand that enlargement is being used as an argument to push this forward, but i warn against any form of junk-timing, because in the candidate countries this can only be seen as a new additional obstacle and as a confirmation of the suspicion - which already exists - that whenever the candidates are close to the goal, we move the goal and move further away. you can't do that! i don't understand what the problem is with the convention. why should the present candidate countries not already participate as new members on an equal and full basis in the decisions on the outcome of the convention? if anyone thinks they can't, they're wrong. let me warn you strongly against the notion that we must now hurry to get our silverware and our precious porcelain to safety before the scum from the east come. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the second the council and parliament. and i'm telling you, that's enough of that. it would be quite senseless to provide much more money, because we have already reached the limit of what we can account for in terms of the deductibility in the candidate countries. it is simply not true that they are treated worse than the current members. in terms of their gross national product, the future new members will receive much more aid from the european budget than the present cohesion countries. so this representation that we are measuring with two different standards is only true if the candidate countries are given a certain preference. but in any case they are not at a disadvantage. the commission has already made a number of proposals. there are dozens of such studies, as we know from the netherlands, that the cost of enlargement would actually be much higher than we had calculated. all these studies, including the dutch ones, have one thing in common: they are based on political assumptions that will not come true. they are, for example, based on negotiated outcomes which have not yet occurred and which will not occur in the future. of course, if we were to negotiate that the entire environmental acquis in the new member states must be achieved by 2005, it would cost 130 billion euros, and then these 130 billion euros would have to be raised. but that's not exactly how we negotiate. we are negotiating in such a way that we remain within the financial possibilities and we are not making any financial commitments for the period after 2006. i do not need to explain to the european parliament that it is not possible to negotiate in such a way as to create financial obligations which are not covered by parliament and the budgetary authority. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we have no financial risk in the expansion. i have my suspicions as to why certain discussions have been initiated in certain member states with certain papers. i am sorry to say that my role as a member of the european commission does not allow me to express my views on this. the fact that the budget of the european union does not allow for tax increases or debt, that we really cannot plan for more than the member states provide us with as contributions, perhaps this should be allowed, and perhaps every finance minister in the european union should know that this is the case. the debate on the beneš decrees has encouraged me to continue the cautious approach taken by the commission to the subject. i ask all those who wish to participate in this discussion to exercise the utmost sensitivity and caution. not all the loud sounds of late have helped us get closer to the goal. i agree with all those who say that there are issues and problems which are not merely negotiable and which should be resolved before we take decisions, because we do not want to see mortgages from the past being dragged into our common european future. - the agenda calls for statements by the council and the commission on the situation and prospects of transatlantic relations, including the import tax on steel.
in writing. - (el) initiatives to introduce community legislation on minimum health and safety requirements in the field of exposure of workers to the risks arising from noise are in the right direction, given the literally wild situation in some member states, such as greece, where the most basic safety measures are lacking. in this context it is important that there are low exposure values that trigger action (83 db); that the responsibility for these measures lies with employers and not with workers, and that hearing protectors should therefore not be taken into account; that the reference period is the working day and not the week, otherwise the state of exhaustion and the exposure of workers to noise is prolonged. these provisions are included in the relevant amendments of the european parliament - and that is why they have my support - whereas they are not provided for in the council's common position. finally, it is important that there are monitoring mechanisms so that these measures are not limited to words.
council. - mr president, the council would like to draw attention to its reply to oral question no 0967/01, in which it refers to article 33 of the treaty on european union which reads: 'this title (title vi, provisions on police and judicial cooperation in criminal matters) shall be without prejudice to the exercise of the responsibilities incumbent upon the member states with regard to the maintenance of law and order and the safeguarding of internal security'. as regards data protection, the 1981 council of europe convention for the protection of individuals with regard to automatic processing of personal data has been ratified by all the member states of the union. the conclusions adopted by the council and the representatives of the governments of the member states on 13 july 2001 on the security of european councils and similar acts recalled the objective of the european union to maintain and develop the union as an area of freedom, security and justice. in this area, citizens should enjoy the right to express their views freely and to assemble peacefully, as provided for in the european convention for the protection of human rights, and to do so in conditions free from threat to their own security, to that of other citizens or to property. the same conclusions underlined the importance of constructive dialogue between organisers of public demonstrations and the authorities of the host country, as well as the importance of maintaining close international contacts, in particular between police and customs authorities, to ensure that legitimate demonstrations are not taken advantage of or abused to commit acts of individual collective violence. as regards mr papayannakis, i would say that the preparatory bodies examining his question met in january and february and worked as required by the treaty, that is to say, with respect for human rights and fundamental freedoms. it will be for the spanish presidency to give the answer it deems appropriate.
- thank you for that. i would like to say that my colleague mr papayannakis is at the conference of delegation chairmen and that is why he is not here. i have seen the president-in-office of the council try to give a very general answer, with some principles, such as the data protection conventions, which are of interest. i would like to ask whether in practice the spanish presidency is preparing not to follow the berlusconi line, which has created too many problems, violated the basic rights of the citizens of the european union, has not allowed greek peace-loving citizens and citizens of other countries to set foot on italian soil, but, i would say, to follow the porto alegre line, that is to say an attempt to create a framework for the legal and peaceful expression of the will and demands of the protesters. i would like to ask him what he has to say about the consultations with the organisers and so on.
council. - mr president, the council would point out to the honourable member that, as regards trade relations with developing countries, it should be remembered that the european union is the world's second largest exporter and first importer of agricultural products and, in particular, that it is by far the world's largest importer of agricultural products from developing countries. the european union alone imports more agricultural products from developing countries than the united states, canada, japan, australia and new zealand combined. between 1997 and 1999, the european union imported more than €35 billion a year of agricultural products from developing countries, with an entry tariff of zero for the most part or very low in a few exceptions. the union has a very active policy in favour of these countries, in particular through two major programmes to promote exports from developing countries to the union. one is the system of generalized preferences and the other is the well-known initiative called eba (everything but arms). the council recalled that, in addition, an agreement on agriculture was concluded within the world trade organisation, which comprises 144 member countries, most of which are developing countries, under the 1994 marrakesh agreements, aimed, inter alia, at reducing subsidies which distort competition on world markets. recently, in november 2001, the development cycle launched by the doha declaration set the goal of further reducing these trade distortions, while helping developing countries to take a more equitable share of global trade. in the context of these new multilateral trade negotiations, the union has committed itself to contributing to the conclusion, by 1 january 2005, of a balanced multilateral agreement to the benefit of all parties. the community's position is to introduce further reductions in export subsidies, such as export credits, state trading enterprises or other types of export monopolies that use one market to subsidise another; or even certain food practices whose real objective is to eliminate surpluses, open markets or suppress competition in markets. at the moment the council does not yet have any proposals from the commission, which, as this parliament knows, is the sole practitioner of the common commercial policy, but when it does, it will examine them in the context of these guidelines, which the commission also shares. there is therefore no risk of any problem between the council and the commission in achieving the objective of giving the greatest advantages to developing countries to export to the union markets.
counsel. - i believe that in the european union - both in the commission, the olaf agency and in the european parliament - the control of all union expenditure has sufficient instruments to control fraud and, of course, i imagine that in the future they will be treated with the same harshness and rigour with which they have been treated in the past. as regards the elimination of export subsidies, i cannot give you an answer as president, because that will be the answer the council will have. i can tell you that spain is against import subsidies, but naturally the council presidency will have to reflect what the council's position is as a whole and i cannot go into that at the moment because we have not yet had any proposal from the commission and the member states have not yet spoken on this matter.
- there is certainly an attempt to change the system of subsidies which has been in place until now under the gatt agreements, and if it is changed, this will be at the expense of agricultural products and the income of poor and middle-class farmers. i should like to say, and i think it should be borne in mind, that 80% of the subsidies go to 20% of the farms and, on the basis of the macsharry report, the subsidies which farmers, particularly those in the south, have received for a long time must be increased to meet the needs of the european union, which is in deficit and imports over 70% of products such as cotton, tobacco, oil, fruit and vegetables and others. secondly, these products are of excellent quality and are for the benefit of the consumers of the european union. i ask the council, because it has not given a clear answer and it has not yet, as it says, taken a position: in any event, will it accept, on the basis of the conclusions of the qatar summit, the abolition of subsidies and if so, will it take into account that in countries such as greece and other countries of the south we will have abandonment of agriculture, with all the consequences that this entails, such as the violent clearing of small and mediumsized farmers, the increase in unemployment and the impact on the environment?
- mr president, commissioner, whenever we discuss the issue of tobacco, i always get the question, we are all against smoking, why should we be against tobacco? the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we are against smoking and we must do everything we can to combat its spread and to reduce it. i'm not objecting. but if we abolish tobacco farming, what we are doing is condemning some european citizens to economic ruin and supporting some tobacco farmers in third countries. what we are going to do is reduce our exports and increase our imports. what we are going to do is increase the unemployed in the european union. is that what this is about? is that our goal? i am not saying that we should not be concerned about the fact that the commission is not prepared to take action in this area. they want to tell us to close down the car factories to fight the deadly accidents on the roads? let us stop being so self-righteous and let us see things as they are and let us deal with things as they should be. let us all sit down together, with an open mind and with realism, and let us put the facts straight so that we can arrive at a strategy for the effective fight against smoking and its harmful effects and a strategy for the development of the european union's economy.
- mr president, commissioner, i should first like to congratulate and thank mr cunha, the rapporteur on the tobacco report, both for his careful and serious approach to the subject and for the sensitivity and understanding he has shown in the speeches of his colleagues from the committee on agriculture. the commission's proposal for a regulation on the common organisation of the market in fruit and vegetables is based on the principle of the free movement of fruit and vegetables. the commission has done no wrong in trying to introduce into the debate political criteria which, apart from being irrelevant to the aspects of the subject under discussion here, are completely unfounded, immature and even provocative, i would say, as regards the general functioning and perspective of the common agricultural policy. it is well known that tobacco growing is a predominantly family-based activity, a highly intensive form of work in which all the members of a family are involved, and that tobacco is grown on barren and poorly fertile land, thus also meeting the need for human presence and activity in areas where desertification and ecological neglect would have incalculable negative social and environmental consequences. these are things known to the commission. the commission has repeatedly stated that it would not accept the amendments proposed by the committee on budgets, and that it would not accept the amendments proposed by the committee on budgets. the commission's proposal for a regulation on the common organization of the market in cereals, which is to be adopted by the council on the basis of article k.3 of the treaty on european union, is a very important one.
- mr president, we would like to express our opposition to any possibility of abolishing or gradually reducing tobacco subsidies, as proposed by the commission, since this would lead to the extinction of european tobacco production. we are opposed to any proposal which accepts the logic of abandoning tobacco growing and is quick to turn the pill into gold by purportedly seeking alternative employment and income solutions for the producers. it is well known that tobacco is grown in disadvantaged areas of the south, mainly mountainous and arid areas, that it is a traditional monoculture and the sole source of income and livelihood for the inhabitants of these areas, for whom there are no alternative employment options. it is outrageous that such proposals should be made when smoking is on the increase, which is covered by 70% by imported tobacco. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the tobacco industry is not left behind, and that the tobacco industry is not left behind. the reductions should concern imports only and not the income of european farmers, for which all appropriate measures should be taken. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the cunha report, despite its positive aspects, accepts, with some reservations, the logic of the reduction of community tobacco production, perhaps in the longer term. this is clear, for example, from amendments nos 7 and 12, which call for encouraging the substitution of other crops for tobacco. that is why we shall not vote in favour of it.
- madam president, i will be extremely brief to commissioner fischler. we have received today the opinion of the council's legal service which challenges the legal basis of recital 5 of the commission proposal. the council's legal service challenges the legality of recital 5. the question to mr fischler is whether he will withdraw recital 5.
in writing. - (el) all the amendments proposed in the report are more positive than the council's proposal for a regulation, and that is why we will vote for them. nevertheless, all the amendments maintain, for a short period, the existing system which the council proposal abolishes. this regime of specific measures, which has been in place for a decade, has not addressed the serious problems of the sector, which is of multidimensional importance for countries where nuts are grown. the main cause of this shortfall is the inadequacy of the specific measures in relation to real needs and, more specifically, in relation to a preferential tariff regime of the community in favour of third countries such as the united states and turkey. as a result, any attempt to improve the nuts sector was undermined by the massive imports of nuts from the united states and turkey at very low prices. since the eu does not want to change this preferential tariff regime in favour of the usa and turkey, it is necessary not only to maintain these specific measures but to substantially strengthen them. instead, the council proposes to repeal them and, in accordance with parliament's amendments, to maintain them for a period. we are totally against the council's proposal because we believe in the upgrading and not the abolition of the specific measures. we are voting in favour of the ep report, despite the limited nature of the measures, and at the same time we are calling for the strengthening of these specific measures and an integrated programme for the development of this sector, which is of multidimensional importance for certain mediterranean regions, and where the eu's dependence is high, at around 50%.
in writing. - (el) the commission proposal for a regulation on tobacco is unacceptable. politically unacceptable, because it is directed against tobacco producers in the name of health protection, whereas the commission has spent billions of euros on products such as red meat or alcohol, which are equally harmful to health. it is socially unacceptable, because it is directed against tens of thousands of tobacco-growing families in the poorest regions of the european union, without the possibility of alternative crops. this is unacceptable at the state level, because the abolition of community tobacco aid will not reduce consumption by even one cigarette. the only effect of this will be to increase the sales of the american multinationals. the committee on agriculture accepted my amendment on the withdrawal of recital 5 which provides for the abolition of tobacco subsidies. it also accepted my amendments on the reintroduction of the mavra-tsebelia premium and on the preparation of a special study on the relationship between community production, imports and consumption of tobacco. although i differ on some points from the resolution of the committee on agriculture, as it is in a positive direction for the maintenance of tobacco cultivation, it has my support.
in writing. - (el) the 'steel crisis' demonstrates in the most convincing way the impasse of the market economy and the liberalisation which the united states and the european union are so stubbornly trying to impose on the whole world. it also shows that the crisis of the capitalist system is so deep that it cannot be dealt with by any management measures or by the "rules of conduct" of the world trade organization (wto) for free trade and free competition. to protect its interests, the united states does not hesitate to violate agreements. the crisis has brought to the fore the contradictions within the world imperialist system and the more general struggle for hegemony between its centres. american imperialism does not hesitate to take measures which reach european imperialism in its economy and the latter, although it closely travels with it and covets its share of the spoils of military adventures, does not hesitate to counterattack. obviously, the demands of the us unions were only a pretext for the bush administration's decision to impose taxes, which was taken against the background of the general crisis affecting the capitalist economy, after the worst year in world trade in twenty-five years and the fall of 38% in us steel imports over the last three years. the workers have nothing to gain from this quarrel. the american workers will no more be able to avoid restructuring and unemployment in the end than the european workers will be able to avoid the spectre of redundancy by going to the wto. what the united states and the european union are trying to save are the profits of the steel industries. but even if they manage to mitigate some of the consequences of the current crisis, another crisis will occur shortly. this phenomenon is characteristic of social systems where the supply to society has long been exhausted, and such a system is represented by capitalism. the only way out is to overturn it, although that seems difficult in the present conditions. crises like the steel crisis offer new opportunities for the struggles of the working class, they can serve to unite, they reveal the hideous face of the capitalist system. the european parliament resolution addresses the problem superficially and attempts to deal with it in the context of liberalisation. it should be noted that the resolution accuses the us of not having carried out the timely restructuring of the steel industry, as the eu did! in other words, it accuses them of not having closed enough enterprises and of not having laid off enough workers, as happened, for example, in the case of the greek steel industry. for this reason, as members of the communist party of greece, we voted against the resolution.
in writing. - (el) once again, a report by the ep, in view of the commission's recommendation on the broad guidelines of economic policy, condemns the anti-social policies of the eu which dismantle, in the interests of monopolies, the acquired rights of workers. workers are once again being called upon to pay the consequences of a recession which is the result of intra-imperialist rivalry, the operation and action of monopolies, and the unbridled pursuit of the increase of capital's profit. the report even raises questions about the implementation of stability and convergence programmes and the coordination of member states in applying anti-social measures 'adapted to the complexity of the global recession'. under pressure from the interests of the monopolies, political decisions are approved and proposed again which lead to a reduction in the cost of labour and social contributions, to violations of collective agreements, of working hours, to the application of flexible forms of work, to lower wages. in the name of the 'demographic challenge of an ageing population', the reform of pension systems, the extension of working life with the extension of the retirement age and the reduction of pensions, in other words the gradual abolition of state obligations in the field of social insurance and the incentive to privatisation in the field of health and pension benefits, are put forward. the weakening of the social security system, combined with the adjustment of working time to the interests of employers, in other words the generalization of parttime work and "flexible" provisions to regulate work, will have the effect of creating a vast camp of "employables", without rights, without claims, subject to the full license of big capital. pressures are mounting to hand over national wealth to private interests, and there are calls for accelerating the pace of privatisation, with liberalisation of the energy, natural gas and transport sectors, as well as privatisation of public utilities and liberalisation of services of general interest, without concern for the impact on workers and consumers. as members of the communist party of greece, we are voting against this report which provides for a guaranteed level of poverty for workers and an optimal profit and prosperity for increasingly powerful big capital, since it aims to suppress the gains of workers and to generalize parttime employment. by denouncing and fighting these policies, the workers will try to avert the coming turmoil, and we will stand by them in their struggles.

in writing. - (el) the communist party of greece welcomes the hundreds of thousands of demonstrators who have expressed their resistance to the eu's policy of poverty, unemployment, privatization, and liberalization. the mobilizations send a message of optimism and demonstrate that the popular movement is expanding its struggle to get rid of this antisocial policy. for their part, eu leaders have declared that poverty and unemployment will be tackled through the development of entrepreneurship, the reduction of state aid, the increase of employability, and the flexibility of work, while they have not failed to announce objectives to accelerate reforms of pension systems and to increase the retirement age by five years by 2010! this is the famous 'social model' which is supposed to bring full employment and make the eu the most dynamic and competitive economy in the world. at this stage, the opposition among eu leaders prevented the completion of the transfer of the energy sector to private capital. however, they did show consensus on the middle east, stating that the palestinian authority as a whole bears responsibility for the fight against terrorism and making recommendations for good conduct to israel. in the face of american threats against iraq and plans to develop new nuclear weapons, their silence was guilty and they were very impatient for the settlement of the outstanding affairs of the 'european army', to take over from nato in the fyrom. the european parliament's resolution is an anthem for anti-social policy, and that is why the members of the european parliament from the communist party of greece voted against it.

- the next item is the report (a5-0047/2002) by mr deprez, on behalf of the committee on civil liberties and human rights, on the draft council decision on security at football matches with an international dimension (c5-0067/2002 - 2001/0824 (cns)).
- mr president, the council's initiative and mr deprez's report fill a large gap in the prevention and suppression of hooliganism and violence in sports stadiums, which has taken on explosive proportions in recent years, partly because of the failure of the member states to implement effective measures. without a doubt, the commercialization of football and the pursuit of profit is the root cause of violent incidents in stadiums. the freaks are no longer caused by wild mobs of impulsive fans, but by organized mobs that use fanaticism and violence to serve specific illegal purposes that have nothing to do with the fan spirit. in short, some have estimated that stadium violence sells, makes a profit. the violence in sports stadiums is now organised and directed and often, with sport as a front, covers up common criminal offences such as drug trafficking and money laundering, according to a british investigation. in recent years, the various hooligan groups have made full use of new technology, mobile phones and the internet, to better organise their activities and, of course, to mislead the police. in this respect, i believe that the creation of a network of information centres and the strengthening of police and judicial cooperation in the 15 member states to combat violence in sports stadiums is a necessary condition for the creation of a european area of freedom and security. the role of sports associations, which must distance themselves from hooliganism and educate their fans in the ideals of noble competition, cooperation, dialogue, respect for differences and peaceful coexistence, is of course also crucial. in conclusion, madam president, i would like to stress that the 2004 olympic games in athens are an important opportunity to revive the olympic spirit and the ideals of olympic truce and peace as an alternative political proposition to reason and the culture of violence.
the commission is not prepared to accept the amendments tabled by the committee on budgets. i would like to thank the commission very briefly for the many contributions, comments and suggestions on - the agenda calls for the recommendation for second reading (α5-0093/2002), by mr schmitt, on behalf of the committee on regional policy, transport and tourism, on the council's common position with a view to adopting a regulation of the european parliament and of the council on common rules in the field of civil aviation and establishing a european aviation safety agency [13382/1/2001 - c5-0696/2001 - 2000/0246 (cod) ].
the commission is aware of the fact that the council has not yet adopted the proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to carcinogens at work. this the establishment of common rules on the contributions in cash or in kind which the states often make to facilitate the establishment of agencies on their territory. the commission has no objection to this. finally, parliament also wanted to send a clear political signal with amendment no 16, which aims to limit the period during which a dual system of community and national certification can be maintained. the commission considers that parliament's arguments are appropriate and therefore we also support the amendment. finally, with regard to amendment no 15, in recital d the commission, like in writing. - (el) the deprez report under consideration is supposed to deal with the problem of violence during international football matches. and we say 'supposedly', because the report and the eu council proposal have nothing to recommend but to oblige the member states to set up national football intelligence offices, in other words 'police centres' which will be responsible for collecting, storing, processing and exchanging information concerning all supporters, both supporters in general and, in particular, supporters at risk. in this way, the real problem of stadium violence is being tackled through police repression and made into a further factor of violation of the individual and democratic rights and freedoms of eu citizens. the increase in violence at national and international sporting events is not due to the lack of intelligence networks and the inadequacy of the mechanisms of repression, but to the commercialisation of sport, the huge economic interests linked to it, the maintenance, as a derivative, of a "sporting" spirit of fanaticism (hooliganism), as well as the development of the psychology of violence, especially among young people. in other words, it is due to situations arising from the very nature of the capitalist system. these serious questions are not even mentioned in the texts of the council and the european parliament under consideration. for the reasons stated above, we as members of the european parliament from the communist party of greece vote against the report.

and the arrival of a ship, since a ship remaining in port sometimes poses a greater risk to safety and pollution for the port itself. i think our own committee on transport is right to say that the crew of a ship should be left to the discretion of the master, who should of course also explain the reasons for a particular decision. amendment no 2 attempts to deal more realistically with the cost of pollution or damage to a host port, so the readiness of ports to receive a ship in distress is reasonable to be strengthened when the port authorities know that there will be a reasonable and immediate compensation for the costs involved in the recovery operation. finally, amendment no 3 is an attempt to provide the necessary resources immediately so that the member states can make the necessary arrangements to provide adequate facilities and equipment in ports of call for the purpose of assisting ships, rescuing them and dealing with pollution. the commission's proposal to allow a period of 18 months is also essential in this respect, so that these proposals are not an option but become law in all the member states of the european union.

commission. – the commission welcomes mr graça moura's report on the commission's communication on an eu strategy towards china. i am very happy to note that overall parliament and the commission have reached similar conclusions on eu-china relations over the past three years and how they should develop in future. despite several differences on some important issues, the eu and china have reached, in the last few years, a wide understanding on areas where the development of their relations is mutually beneficial. this has been amply demonstrated by the successful annual summits in 2000 and 2001, and, most recently, by mr patten's official visit to china which i will report on in the following remarks. he was extremely well received, as a representative of the eu, wherever he went and had intensive meetings with president jiang zemin, the deputy prime minister, qian qichen, five senior ministers and other policy makers. all of his interlocutors spoke frankly and openly about eu-china relations. some strong messages he received during the visit were: a recognition of the increasing role of the eu in the world, an appreciation for the recent, rapid progress in eu-china relations; and a genuine will and commitment to continue the development of eu-china relations and cooperation at all levels. during his visit, mr patten put across the messages that all eu member states were committed to the eu's china policy, that not only had trade and investment cooperation been impressive, but also that very strong progress was being achieved in other bilateral and transnational areas of concern, for example immigration, the environment and science and technology. dialogues on regional and international issues, such as the korean peninsula, asem, afghanistan, and international terrorism were proving to be increasingly beneficial to both sides. returning to the report, i note that it rightly picks out the importance of the events of 11 september and the launch of the fight against international terrorism, and china's entry into the wto. these events will help define, to some extent, the parameters for eu-china relations in the international arena for the foreseeable future. more specifically, like parliament, the commission welcomes china's support in the international fight against terrorism and china’s undertaking to assist in the reconstruction of afghanistan. the report congratulates china on its accession to the wto and expresses support for china's continuing economic and social reforms. we agree with this. it took china 15 long years of arduous negotiations to join the wto. china also came a long way in that period. from being a rather isolated, inward-looking, centrally planned economy with a slightly open door, it is about to remove – we hope – many of the remaining great barrier walls to free trade. it is therefore that it has joined the wto, but we will now need to ensure that china fully adheres to its commitments. we also welcome the fact that wto accession will accelerate reform, but are conscious that the required reforms will bring with them serious challenges because of unemployment, urban drift and so on. we will do all we can to help to assist china during this delicate transition. the human rights situation in china has, in some respects, improved in the last few years, notably in the field of social and economic rights. but we remain deeply concerned about the general human rights situation. we remain committed to the human rights dialogue. however, while we are conscious that the way ahead will be a long-term process, we nevertheless believe that improvements in political and civil rights are needed in china, also in the short-term. chris patten raised human rights issues in detail during his visit, particularly with foreign minister tang. the report draws the attention of the chinese government to the importance of respecting and protecting the religious and cultural heritage of tibet, and the identity of the tibetan people. chairman re di, the leader of the delegation from the national people's congress of the tibetan autonomous region that visited parliament last month – whom some of you met and also referred to today – gave assurances that the chinese government pays a lot of attention to the protection of minorities and to their religious freedoms. we attach great importance to these issues and were happy to note these assurances, but we will of course continue to be vigilant about tibet and will continue to call for a direct dialogue between beijing and the dalai lama. mr patten also discussed these issues with foreign minister tang and vice-premier qian qichen. as regards the issue of our cooperation with china i will take the liberty of informing mr dell'alba that we are cooperating at a level of about eur 50 million annually. we started from a traditional situation where we were funding quite a wide range of different activities. we are now at a point that is quite important, having approved in the commission on 1 march this year a country strategy paper which brings our cooperation with china into the same mainstream as our global development policy, and which, is the responsibility of the commissioner responsible for development cooperation. the future priorities of our cooperation in china will be support for the social economic reform process, sustainable development and environment, and the promotion of good governance, human rights and the rule of law. we have singled out these issues from an analysis of what is needed in order to use our basic general principles of fighting poverty in a manner that fits china's situation. alongside the different aspects i have singled out here, there are several other issues to which we attach great importance, such as relations with hong kong, macao and taiwan, and international issues such as global warming and illegal migration that, unfortunately, i do not have sufficient time to comment on today. however, i welcome the report's reconfirmation that the eu has a profound interest in a stable, prosperous and open china, which embraces democratic practices, free market principles, human rights and the rule of law, and that the eu must pursue policies which will help china further its endeavours in this direction. that is what we fully intend to do through our increased dialogues and cooperation across the board. lastly, i would like to reiterate the observation that for china some of our desired goals will take longer to achieve than others. as ever, there is a chinese proverb that neatly encapsulates this very point, which is: "you won't help shoots grow by pulling them up higher".
- thank you, commissioner. the debate is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow at 12 noon.
- the report (a5-0071/2002) by mr fatuzzo, on behalf of the committee on employment and social affairs, on the commission communication entitled 'an integrated approach to national strategies for safe and sustainable pensions' (com (2001) 362 - c5-0012/2002 - 2002/2017 (cos)) is on the agenda.
- thank you very much, mr fatuzzo. everyone knows that you have a brilliant record in explaining votes!
- mr president, mr commissioner, i think that the dear mr fatuzzo must have a great day today as the outstanding representative of the pensioners. indeed, the main purpose of today's report is to explore ways in which we can support national pension systems with three main objectives: preserving their ability to achieve the social objectives set, maintaining long-term economic viability and responding to the changing needs of society. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. in the view of our political group, therefore, state-owned collective pension schemes continue to take precedence over purely private schemes and remain one of the pillars of wealth redistribution. this is why funding from the social partners remains necessary. i therefore think that the amendments proposed by the ppe are upsetting this basis and the balance achieved in the committee on employment and social affairs. in fact, proposing that only health care and social protection be financed from the state budget, while workers' pensions be secured only by contributions from workers and employers, overturns any logic of solidarity. the call also for member states to take into account in their strategies the positive effects of a reduction in the pension burden of contributions to labour costs, since an increase in the percentage of contributions to pension subsidiary funds would in practice mean shifting the entire pension burden onto workers. on the contrary, the aim of the reform should be to widen the base of contributors and to broaden alternative forms of financing. the proposal also for democratic legitimization of the reform through a more active role of the european parliament with opinions on the national strategy reports and the definition of indicators to determine the results, but also through the expansion of social dialogue with the participation of representatives of pensioners, non-governmental organizations, and women's organizations is a key prerequisite for the success of the reform project. i therefore think that amendment no 17 should be withdrawn.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, there is no doubt that the subject we are discussing today is at the heart of the major challenges facing the european union in modernising its social model and achieving the lisbon objectives. our responsibilities at european and national level for achieving these objectives and reforming pension systems are also great. the committee on employment of our parliament, madam commissioner, supports your efforts to highlight the importance of this reform with its economic aspects, in addition to the social ones, and the initiatives to coordinate pension policies with common objectives, working methods, monitoring and evaluation of progress. we believe that these policies should be consistent and complementary with the policies of coordination on employment, social exclusion and poverty. given the scope and importance of these policies for the present and the future of europe, they must be democratic in nature and involve not only the social partners but also social and regional bodies and women's organisations, since these social reforms are particularly relevant to women and this method of open coordination must guarantee the full participation of the european parliament. but that's not all. the necessary reforms must be supported by the whole of society to be sustainable. the public is unprepared and uninformed. i think it is imperative that information campaigns be organised in cooperation between the european commission and national governments and the social partners. this is particularly important for member states that are reluctant or do not dare to make the necessary reforms, but also for the preparation of the candidate countries. i would like to congratulate our rapporteur, mr fatuzzo, on his serious and thorough work, which we will support.
the council. - mr president, the question concerns the belgian proposal to include in the descriptions of the sis (schengen computerised system) persons known to have committed acts of recognised disorder and who may become rioters at certain events, such as sporting, social, cultural events or any kind of demonstration. the purpose was to assimilate this information to descriptions under article 99 of the schengen convention, although the purpose would be different: that is, to prevent the participation of these persons in certain events, rather than carrying out specific or discrete controls as, for example, have recently taken place. the ministers discussed this proposal at the joint committee meeting on 7 december 2001. there were differences of opinion and some delegations were strongly opposed to the proposal. consequently, no detailed examination of the proposal at expert level has been carried out and no further details have been given as to the possible conditions for the introduction of such data on these persons should it be agreed to introduce them. so at the moment there is no definitive answer to the question but, given the current views, it does not seem likely that this proposal will succeed in the near future and even less likely that it will affect people who have not committed crimes. for information, the council notes that there is a draft council decision on security at international football matches. the draft decision provides for the exchange of police information between national football information points but does not envisage the use of sis for this purpose.
i think i have been quite explicit, mr sjöstedt. the council has not been receptive to the idea of entering into the sis lists of persons known only for their activities in various events which are out of the ordinary, because this would mean an absolutely intolerable monitoring of persons who have not committed any crime. the sis only collects lists of criminals. if there is to be a disturbance of public order at sporting or political events or demonstrations, as has happened in the past, discreet control of the entry of people and of borders is much more effective than making these classifications which are not only odious but contrary to our whole civic tradition. i should like to say that the last time discreet checks were carried out was at the barcelona european council, to prevent the streets of that city from becoming a battlefield, as happened in genoa, and, by temporarily suspending the schengen convention, this allowed the spanish police to stop at the border some troublemakers who, for example, were carrying hammers and baseball bats to break windows. over 2,000 were requisitioned. all persons were let in except those carrying instruments for acts of destruction. these controls are certainly more effective than the publication of a list of persons who, theoretically, have not committed any crime and who therefore do not need to be classified.

other colleagues have said this - or even by informing and teaching children because of course the ban leaves behind the idea and the charm of the secret. i think that all of this is not enough, especially since these issues and these programmes, now with the internet, will be available to the whole world today or tomorrow, commissioner. i think that we need some european legislation and some regulation that is stronger than what we have now. i'm not a big believer in self-regulation because competition has its own hard-and-fast rules. however, it is not wrong to imagine a code of conduct that is somewhat more binding than it is today. we must give both parents and consumers the right to lay siege to the producers of these abominations. maybe it's a little counterbalance to the competition. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the programmes are broadcast in the same way as the programmes themselves, and that the programmes are broadcast in the same way as the programmes themselves. however, if we do not find something more original and imaginative, i remain pessimistic, mr president, about the future and the prosperity of this type of programme.
- mr president, the conference on the ageing of the population is certainly very important, since it is expected to provide policy guidelines to guarantee the rights of the elderly, their quality of life and, above all, the fight against discrimination. as commissioner anna diamantopoulou said in madrid on monday, the fact that people are living longer and in better health offers the prospect of a new model of life, a new society, where the achievements of technology and science will be reflected in a positive way. however, that is not the case today. instead, the cassandras of all kinds are daily advancing their ominous prophecies, about the planet sinking under the weight of 6 billion people, about the resources of production being depleted, about the natural environment being destroyed, about the poor peoples growing and multiplying as we the developed ones grow smaller and smaller and older, while our social security systems collapse. the solution proposed by the malthusian theory is to reduce births in third world countries but increase ours. that's the morality of the developed world. my point is that the imbalances in the distribution of population and the demographic issue are directly linked to the imbalances in the development and distribution of the planet's resources, to the issues of social justice, education, gender equality, human rights, development standards. the future of the planet is certainly not threatened by hungry children in the third world, nor by an aging population, but by the consumption patterns and lifestyles of the developed one third of the earth's population. that is where our intervention and the new demographic policy is needed. however, until we have this comprehensive policy of sustainable development that the commissioner has mentioned, we can follow the good advice of those who propose to abandon the restrictive immigration policy and give full social and political rights to immigrants. the populations and labour force of sweden and germany would have shrunk if they had not pursued policies of integration of immigrants and full recognition of women's labour rights. the strengthening of these two policies may temporarily provide short-term solutions to the problems. the long-term solutions, however, are about a fair, different system of economic and social development.
in writing. - (el) pension rights are treated not as needs of the working classes, which must be met under the responsibility of the state, but as an "indicator", an "expenditure" contrary to the objectives of the stability pact and the increase in the profits of big capital. through the "achievement of social objectives" and the maintenance of financial sustainability, a policy is introduced which abolishes the social and social insurance rights of workers, with a view to reducing the cost of labour. the reform of social security in the context of capitalist restructuring aims to abolish labour rights, develop flexible forms of employment, commercialise health and social security, while the reserve funds of the funds will be risked on the financial markets. under the pretext of reducing government spending and tackling the consequences of the demographic problem, which is the responsibility of the anti-social system and policies, conquests and fundamental rights are being abolished, the retirement age is being raised, working life is being extended after a working life, pensions are being converted into social benefits, and at the same time the problem of unemployment is worsening and part-time work is spreading. it is hypocritical and demagogic to talk about the dignity and autonomy of the elderly, since 70% of pensioners live below the poverty line, and it is hypocritical to talk about gender equality, since it paves the way for raising the retirement age for women and for abolishing the right to early retirement, maternity protection, etc. we, on the other hand, support a social security system that meets the needs of the people, with public social security and free public health care for all, a lowering of the retirement age to 60 for men, 55 for women and 50-55 for hard and unhealthy occupations, a reduction in the number of years of contributions and a stable, full-time job.
in writing. - (el) under the heading 'strengthening cooperation with third countries in the field of higher education' is hidden the enterprise which is being launched with a view to exporting the european union's educational model to the candidate countries for accession, to the other countries of the european continent but also to the third world countries. symptomatically, the report under review emphasises the development of 'an internationally competitive higher education system', which makes it very clear that it views education as a commodity subject to the laws of the market, liberalisation and competition. even more outrageous, the report advocates the introduction of a european university quality label (!), as if education were an electrical appliance or a washing machine on the department store shelf. the report of the parliamentary committee is better than the commission's text on this point. the report also reiterates the well-known positions - mobility of students and teachers, joint study programmes and even diplomas, recognition of teaching modules, training courses and diplomas - in the name of which harmonisation and a bottom-up approach to university studies and a general devaluation of education are imposed. the report does not, of course, fail to recall the bologna and prague declarations which are the spearhead of the intervention in the education systems of european countries, whether they are members of the european union or not. the communist party of greece is fighting for a general revalorisation of higher education, for the strengthening of its public and free character, against the commercialisation and conversion of educational institutions to entrepreneurship. for these reasons, as members of the european parliament from the communist party of greece, we voted against the report.

- mr. president, mrs. berger has done a lot of work and i think she's mastered the subject. the purpose of the directive is to establish a single legal framework for the distance marketing of financial services to consumers, supplementing existing sectoral rules and filling the gaps created by the general directive on the distance marketing of consumer goods. this directive facilitates the functioning of the internal market and provides for greater protection of consumers. the question of maximum or minimum harmonization is a very delicate one, and you have heard that there are some ambiguous views. i have the impression that a solution can be found if at least some special arrangements are passed or allowed to be introduced on certain matters. the maximum harmonisation solution, combined with the commission's obligation to provide information to suppliers on the different national regulations, in some respects particularly helps smes providing financial services because it reduces the costs of informing suppliers and increases transparency. the principle was also retained on the important issue of the right of withdrawal, with the exception only of life insurance and old age pensions. the 14 day deadline is a compromise between the 7 days originally proposed by the commission and the 30 days requested by parliament. the method of calculating time limits is also regulated in a uniform manner. the uniform treatment of the right of withdrawal enhances clarity and legal certainty in the internal market. a very crucial point to note is how the common position clarifies that the directive under discussion should take into account the e-commerce directive 2031 as regards the changes it regulates. the identification of the country of origin is a central point and should not be overlooked. in any event, despite the remaining imperfections, it is gratifying that the common position will largely satisfy the european parliament's positions. i agree with the rapporteur that the directive should enter into force immediately and that is why we must avoid the delay caused by amendments to individual regulations.
- mr president, mr bouwman has been very sensitive and has successfully worked out a compromise solution, and we congratulate him on that. the bouwman report was adopted by a large majority in parliament's first reading. the european parliament welcomed the commission's initiative, as it completed the reform of three main directives - the other two concerned the protection of employees against collective redundancies and the other the transfer of undertakings - which aimed to reduce the insecurity of workers in the face of structural change. this common objective cannot be confined to these initiatives. if we want to achieve reforms in the european union and achieve the lisbon objective of a more dynamic economy, we must, with the same increased interest, deal with the insecurities arising from work, deal with the phobias with reforms and not go against them. it is the only way to prevent the renationalization of community policies and the consolidation of far-right the universal protection of wages, whether it be a basic salary, allowances or various increases. a question arose about the inclusion of compensation in the event of the employee's dismissal. the council's clarification that it does not refuse to include these guarantees, but that it considers them to be included in the guaranteed benefits as soon as they are derived from the employment relationship, is of course an acceptance in substance. however, in my personal opinion, the express reference to the term 'compensation' should have been included, as is the case in the international pe-de), draftsman of the opinion of the committee on employment and social affairs. - i know . mr president, i would agree with the rapporteur, mrs torres marques, and with mr de clercq that the tourism sector, despite the crisis in the world and its impact on europe, is the sector with the greatest potential for creating new jobs. the committee on employment and social affairs believes that we must make the most of this advantage, both at european and national level. firstly, member states should include specific commitments for employment in the tourism sector in their national employment action plans. the main focus of these actions should be on vocational training, improving the quality of jobs and the need to implement policies to protect workers in seasonal, temporary and precarious employment. the commission has also proposed that the member states should be able to apply the principle of subsidiarity to all the measures which they adopt in the field of public health. the commission has already taken steps to ensure that the community's tourism sector is not left behind. the committee on transport and tourism has therefore asked the european commission to draw up a study of the market needs in cooperation with the member states and within the framework of a tourism strategy designed at national and regional level. it also called on the european commission, in cooperation with cedefop, to propose standards for monitoring the training of staff and to promote the necessary synergy between training and work experience. to exploit this potential of tourism, all these policies should be accompanied by support for human capital mobility, both at national and european level. tourism is a key sector in which such mobility policies can be inspired. we also believe that we must exploit the potential for job creation and to stimulate the employment of women;
