the commission - mr president, commissioner, mrs torres marques has done a very good job and, roughly speaking, i would say that the messages that emerge from this report are three. the first is that we must eventually move from words to deeds. i have seen discussions like this before in this chamber of the european parliament, and at some point they begin to resemble a ritual devoid of meaning. we must therefore really move from discussions to action, to measures, to the implementation of what we are talking about. the second is that we should obviously welcome the european commission's communications on tourism, but tell commissioner liikanen that they are not enough. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. there are measures by various directorates-general, not just yours, concerning tourism, the internal market, the structural funds and the environment. these measures should be coordinated, despite the absence of a common policy, in order to produce the best possible european union policy on tourism. the third point i would like to make, mr president, is the famous legal basis for tourism. there is a legal basis for industry. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the commission's proposal for a regulation on the protection of the environment is a very important one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done in this area. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done on this subject.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, a few weeks ago the greek minister for health felt obliged to ask for the suspension of the operation of schools at all levels of education because of a viral infection that had been reported. schools were closed for only two days, and very quickly the case rate dropped to zero. however, the impact on greek tourism was immediate and significant, with several cancellations of trips exacerbating the crisis due to the well-known events of september. this reaction was not justified, of course, since the ministry's measure was purely preventive, and showed seriousness and responsibility, a fact that should have reassured the tourist. however, the whole psychology created by certain media and some factors led to cancellations disappointing those who live from tourism in greece. but what else could the minister of health do? should he consider first the harm to the businessman or the possible risk, however small, to the health of the local and the visitor? what would you do, mr commissioner, as minister? i guess the same. you'd put your health first. but it's not the fault of the businessman and anyone who makes a living from tourism. it is right to ask for the right treatment, to ask for the protective umbrella of the european union. when he is active and serves a sector which contributes so much to the balanced development of the regions of the union, that is, ultimately to european integration, but at the same time in a sector which is so fragile, so sensitive to all kinds of crises, then that entrepreneur, that worker, that european citizen considers it right and logically necessary not to be left to his own devices. it must be possible to take measures to mitigate such adverse effects, it must be possible to finance appropriate measures from the structural funds, and a specific budget line must be provided to improve tourism, with all that this means and implies. it is now clear that the tourism dimension must be integrated into all community decision-making processes. the very good report by mrs torres marques points the way in this direction.
- mr president, i should first like to warmly congratulate the commissioner on her initiative to study the european union's particular challenges facing its young people today, to consult them and to proceed with a new cooperation strategy which regards young people as the key asset and force for the course and development of the european union. the white paper is indeed an opportunity for all politicians, at european and national level, to realise our responsibility towards this objective. in our self-criticism, we must therefore recognise that we lack the attitudes and practices that prepare young people to be active citizens in their national and local societies and, at the same time, responsible european citizens of tomorrow. it is true that persistent unemployment and the resulting social problems, the lack of quality in education and training, the uncertainty about the future, frustrate and alienate young people from society. at the same time, as studies show, they are looking for new social relationships and new forms of intervention. the rapporteur is taking the right approach in this respect and we warmly thank her. at the same time, in most countries, the structures and institutional framework do not exist to enable young people to participate in the life of society in a concrete and meaningful way. in this area there are different experiences in the countries of the european union, which have to do with the different administrative and regional organization, but also the political tradition. the promotion of good practice will be absolutely useful in this case. but also at european level, commissioner, give young people in europe greater opportunities to meet, to work together, to show solidarity and to prepare for their common future. despite the range of european student exchange programmes, for example, there is a great gap in education systems. in the recognition of degrees, some universities are seeking to make it more difficult for students from other european union countries to be admitted, rather than welcoming them and bringing them into contact with their own student community. the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for adoption.
- mr. president, the youth is the most vulnerable social group. it is therefore quite natural that the crisis phenomena of the capitalist system should strike the young first. really, what does capitalist society have to offer to youth? a society where everything is sold and everything is bought. what, in particular, does european union policy have to offer? an education system incapable of providing meaningful knowledge, which substitutes training for education, which excludes a significant proportion of young people on the basis of class criteria. the commission has already taken the initiative in the field of social security, and i am sure that the commission will be able to support the amendments tabled by the committee on social affairs and employment. individualism and competition instead of collective action and militant demand. the television subculture, the sport of the hooligans, the fake drug paradise. and when young people dare to claim, sometimes in my country it happens that they drag themselves into the courts. the commission's white paper, while identifying some problems, does not go into the causes or offer solutions. it is mainly concerned with integrating young people, leading them to directives that are painless for the system, but without a way out for the young. it highlights the vacuum of the european ideal, the participation in the institutions of the system, which young people have rejected on the basis of their experience and intuition. it also promotes the so-called volunteering as the ultimate form of social solidarity, and even entrepreneurship as an antidote to unemployment. it downplays the serious drug problem by taking an almost neutral position. the attempt to exonerate the system, to convince that there is a way out of its own framework, is obvious. for the communist party of greece, there is no way out for the youth without questioning the foundations of the class system. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of young people against the dangers of exposure to asbestos is a welcome one.

commission. - mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the second reading by the european parliament is a decisive step in the procedure for adopting the framework programme under the codecision procedure. i would like to emphasize that the cooperation between the three institutions during this procedure has been particularly fruitful. i thank parliament and the council for their commitment. i would like to thank in particular not only the rapporteur, mr caudron, but also the non-existent rapporteurs, mr van velzen, mrs plooij-van gorsel, mr piétrasanta and mr alyssandrakis, and the chairman of the itre committee, mr westendorp, and all the members of the committee and collaborators who have contributed to it. i would also like to pay tribute to the spanish presidency and to minister marimón for the very efficient way in which they have managed this important and delicate phase of the adoption process. i would also like to stress the importance of the rapid adoption of the framework programme and its launch in january 2003 for researchers and the scientific community in europe. on behalf of the commission, i can agree to all the compromise amendments voted by the itre committee. the rapporteur, mr gérard caudron, has outlined the main points on which compromise amendments have been proposed by the itre committee and how the council has indicated that it can take them into account at the end of a series of informal trilogue meetings. i shall simply make a few comments on each of these points, and, if necessary, give you the information you do not already have. the first aspect is the technical amendments voted on by the itre committee on 23 april, which are to be incorporated into the specific programmes. the commission undertakes to include them in the specific programmes by including the following statement in the council minutes: 'the commission considers that the amendments voted by the itre committee on 23 april 2002 in a block vote in relation to the second reading of the sixth framework programme are broadly acceptable and that, subject to the necessary stylistic changes, they can be incorporated in the decisions of specific programmes implementing the sixth framework programme and, where necessary, in the rules for the participation of undertakings, research centres and universities in the framework programme'. the council intends to make a statement along the same lines. secondly, the question of the instruments for implementation and the participation of smes. the formula agreed by the three institutions underlines the need to ensure a smooth transition between the current and the new framework programmes, in line with parliament's concerns. it is also clearly stated that special measures will be taken to stimulate and facilitate the participation of small and medium-sized enterprises and research entities in the candidate countries in the actions carried out in the priority thematic areas. health, then. in line with parliament's wishes, the aspects of health research have been both strengthened and better identified, particularly as regards the european dimension of research and the transfer of the results of research to patients. this is particularly true of cancer research, which, as you have been told, is now the subject of a specific action with substantial resources. another point is electronic networks for research. parliament expressed its concern that the activities carried out on this theme in the framework of infrastructure support activities should be implemented in a manner consistent with those carried out in the relevant priority thematic area. to remove any ambiguity on this point, it was agreed at the trilogue that the commission would have the following statement entered in the council minutes: 'the commission declares that the resources allocated to activities in the field of high-speed electronic networks, in particular géant and grid, which total eur 300 million, including up to eur 100 million for thematic priority 2 - information society technologies - and up to eur 200 million for the research infrastructures part, will be managed in an integrated manner.' the sense of responsibility with which the three institutions dealt with this matter is also evident in the rapid agreement reached on the overall amount. however, significant changes in the budgetary breakdown have been made in the direction desired by parliament. in addition to the increase in funds for health research, the increase in funds for specific aspects of international cooperation and the theme of science and society, i believe, correctly reflects the importance attached by your institution to these aspects. international cooperation: in this area, as parliament wished, additional emphasis was placed on specific measures to support international cooperation with developing countries, mediterranean countries, russia and the newly independent states. in addition, clarification was given on the different ways of supporting international cooperation in the framework programme. ethics, well, this question oh, how sensitive ethics is. as you know, it has been very difficult to find a solution on this point which would reconcile the wishes and constraints of the three institutions. i would remind you that my aim is not, of course, to provide for the harmonization of ethical rules at european level. the commission has only the desire to advance european research, while respecting the prerogatives of each member state and its parliament: no wonder in a field so characterized by cultural and philosophical diversity and where the line of conduct has always been, and will always remain, respect for opinions, values and sensitivities, within the limits of universally accepted principles. it is nevertheless necessary to specify the conditions under which research in the field of life sciences and technologies could be carried out in the specific framework of community programmes. to this end, and i believe this is an important step, the commission has had the following statement entered in the council minutes: 'in accordance with the opinion of the european parliament on the proposal for the sixth framework programme and taking into account the opinion of the european group on ethics in science and new technologies, the commission considers that research in the following areas should not be funded under the framework programme: research activities for human cloning for reproductive purposes, research activities aimed at altering the genetic heritage of human beings which could make these human modifications heritable, research activities aimed at creating human embryos for research purposes only or for the provision of stem cells, in particular by transfer of somatic cell nuclei. " the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the animal testing of animals is not restricted to the use of animals in research. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, in a short time, thanks to exemplary cooperation - and i thank all those who have contributed - we have come a long way. once the vote in plenary is confirmed, as i hope, once the consensus is reached, we will not be at the end of the road. the decision on the framework programme is only the first part of a system which also includes the rules for participation, which are also adopted under the codecision procedure, and the specific programmes. in order to achieve the objectives set, the adoption of the framework programme and its implementing instruments by parliament and the council in june requires the three institutions to continue the close consultation work they have been carrying out successfully since the beginning of the procedure. a very intensive and fruitful cooperation was established with the rapporteur for the rules of participation, mrs quisthoudt-rowohl, and with the rapporteurs for the specific programmes, mr van velzen, mr alyssandrakis, mr piétrasanta and mr schwaiger, and mrs zorba. the effort thus initiated must not be slackened. the commission intends to continue to do everything in its power to promote interinstitutional dialogue. for the first time in the history of community research, we are in a position to adopt the framework programme before the last possible moment, in time for it to be launched and implemented under the best possible conditions. let us seize this opportunity and give ourselves the means to exploit this possibility to the full. researchers and european citizens alike will be grateful. (applause) i would like to thank
the council. - mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, this week we have reached a high point in the process of discussion to secure the adoption of the sixth framework programme, a process which began under the swedish presidency, has continued under the belgian presidency and has been resumed under the spanish presidency. parliament has played a very active role in this process, through the caudron report, already at the first reading of the framework programme. we have taken many aspects of that reading in our common position. and they have been taken because the very issues, the concerns that have arisen in parliament were also issues and concerns that have arisen in the council and that have also arisen in other forums of discussion. i would naturally like to thank the chairman of the committee on industry, foreign trade, research and energy, mr caudron and the alternate rapporteurs, mr van velzen, mr piétrasanta and mr plooij-van gorsel, for a really excellent cooperation work which has the merit of being thoroughly integrated into the programme. i am convinced that the programme which will emerge from this process - and i am fully confident that we will define the final programme tomorrow - is a substantially better programme than the first programme which was presented to us and is therefore a bet on maturity and collaboration between the various institutions, in the common position on the first reading but even more so in this stimulating process which we have had during our presidency. i think that parliament and the council have come very close to our positions, and we have done so in response to the request of scientists and companies that we should put this great instrument into operation now and in response to the mandate given to us at the barcelona council that we should make every effort to adopt the sixth framework programme within our presidency, and also - as i said - because the concerns have been very common. the specific aspects of the content have been developed further, such as the health science research topics, the question of a larger geant network, the science and society topics, research, support for environmental issues, etc. that is why i say that in terms of content we are now offering a much better defined framework programme which will serve as the centrepiece of another major initiative which goes beyond the framework programme, which is the development of the european research and innovation area. the barcelona council, taking up the lisbon commitment, made a very ambitious commitment that by 2010 europe should reach an investment expenditure on research and development of 3% of gross domestic product, with a substantial share, two thirds, of the private sector of enterprises and the private sector as a whole. but it is not so much a question of a number as of recognising that europe must change the trend and make a qualitative and quantitative leap to achieve this objective of being a highly competitive society in the knowledge society. that is why the sixth framework programme is an essential part of it. but with the successful development of the sixth framework programme we will also develop other initiatives, such as greater coordination of national programmes, a task which we have promoted during our presidency. it is therefore essential that in this particular process we respond effectively to the expectations of the scientific community and of business and that we reserve the right, as has been done, to have small and mediumsized enterprises actively involved. i therefore thank you once again for the work you have done and i am sure that the other aspects - such as the rules for participation in the specific programmes - will be defined once the framework programme has been defined. for our part, the council has fully supported the initiatives of the presidency - and we are very pleased about that - in this process of discussions we have had. in particular, the council takes note of the commission's view and supports the presidency in including all the amendments tabled as a whole, as far as possible, finding the appropriate wording within the specific programmes. the council as a whole supports this whole fruitful process of discussion that we have had.

- the agenda calls for the presentation by the commission of the preliminary draft general budget for the financial year 2003.
the commission. - mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i have to say that i am very pleased with the way the commission has been working. on 30 april the commission adopted the preliminary draft budget for 2003. i am pleased to be able to present to you today the general outline of this preliminary draft budget for 2003. let me first explain the general figures for the 2003 budget. we had the opportunity to speak briefly about this last night in the committee on budgets. the commission's preliminary draft budget is eur 98.2 billion. this represents an increase of 2.7% over the current budget. this rate of increase is lower than the average rate of increase of the member states compared with the projected rates of increase of the public budgets of the member states. i would also like to stress, first of all, that the preliminary draft budget is thus eur 4.7 billion below the maximum amount decided in the financial planning of agenda 2000. i think it is also good news that, despite the many new requirements, a preliminary draft budget can be drawn up in such a way that there is still a large margin for unforeseen events and that this can then be taken into account in the financial planning. in terms of the percentage of gross national income, the budget is 1.03%, which means that the european government's share will be 1.03% next year. that's a very small state quota. it's lower than in previous years. i stress this because it shows that budgetary discipline is also taken very seriously at european level. i also stress this because in the public debate it is often said that we will have these and those developments which will blow up the budget. no, it is not, but we have again a preliminary draft budget below the ceilings set as ceilings in the financial planning. above all, we should also make clear what is financed by this government quota of just over one per cent of the eu's economic output within the european budget, and into which policy areas this taxpayer money is going. first, the agricultural sector. this is still the most important area. the total amount of expenditure on agricultural policy is eur 48.7 billion. this figure includes, for example, aid to the candidate countries, i.e. to farmers in the candidate countries, and it also includes staff in the relevant directorate-general. in other words, if we convert this into a percentage of the eu's gross national income, it is just under 0.5% of the eu's gross national income that is to be used for agricultural expenditure through the european budget. the presentation i have just made of the agricultural sector is in line with the new approach to the division of the budget by policy areas. in the past, it was very often the case that citizens could not read the budget - if there was any access at all - because it only listed the budget lines, the legal bases, the political content, so to speak, what the money was intended to achieve, but not. this has now been done with the new budget structure and it is easy to see at a glance how much is to be spent on which policies. to mention a few figures before moving on to other topics: regional policy is allocated eur 21.8 billion, fisheries eur 940 million, external relations eur 3.2 billion, development eur 1.1 billion, employment and social affairs eur 9.8 billion and the promotion of the information society alone eur 1 billion. this is a much more meaningful breakdown than the simple breakdown by financial planning categories. i would like to go into these categories, however. i have already mentioned the agricultural sector. the commission proposal foresees a margin of eur 2.3 billion for agriculture, which means that the commission's preliminary draft budget is eur 2.3 billion below the maximum amount foreseen in the financial plan for agriculture. this positive result is the result of a combination of several factors: on the one hand, the forecast of relatively favourable agricultural market developments, on the other hand, the situation of relatively low stocks and an underlying euro-dollar parity of 1 euro = 0.88 dollar. i must point out once again that this is not the commission's forecast of the future development of the euro-dollar parity, but that the rule is that when drawing up the preliminary draft budget we must take as a basis the average of the previous months. but i would point out that if the euro-dollar parity changes, in other words if the euro rises, agricultural spending will also rise. but this is not a risk for the budget, because the large margin that remains can then absorb this. we will present the so-called mid-term review in the agricultural sector to the commission in june. mr fischler will then give an overview of the way in which agricultural expenditure is likely to develop in the future and will put forward proposals for reform. however, this will not directly affect the 2003 financial year. i would point out that we have different rates of increase in agricultural policy. on the one hand, as in recent years, following the commission's proposal, the entire amount of the financial planning should be used for rural development, and this instrument will continue to play a very important role in the future and will certainly receive special attention in the mid-term review of the agricultural reform, because it is necessary to promote alternative employment opportunities with rural development measures. first of all, this category includes environmental measures in agricultural policy and is therefore of great importance for sustainable agricultural development. in the context of market measures and direct aids to the agricultural policy, i would like to point out that 2003 will be the first year in which the new agricultural market organization for goats and sheep will apply. that means that we have a slight increase in this area, because the decision is that a premium of 21 euros per animal will be paid for sheep, for example. this is an increase compared to before. the commitment appropriations for structural operations - now i come to structural policy - are in line with the current financial planning. however, the budgetary ceiling is slightly exceeded, since the commission, in accordance with the decision of the european parliament and the european council, has decided to present the second tranche of eur 27 million for the special measures for the conversion of the fishing fleet in spain and portugal. the commission's proposal is to finance this from the flexibility reserve, and i think this is in line with the agreement contained in last year's resolution. in the case of payment appropriations for structural policy as a whole, the estimation of needs over the past two years has been very difficult because of the start-up phase of the programmes. we had under-implementation here in 2000 and 2001. some of the funds were reprogrammed. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. in particular, a large part of this is also intended for the final payment for the old programmes implemented by the member states in the years 1994-99. there have been delays in all member states and, consequently, delays in final payments. we hope that all final payments will be made by then in 2003. i would now like to turn to category 3, i.e. internal policy measures, which is a wide range. the proposed budget is eur 6.7 billion and leaves a reserve of eur 81 million, which will certainly be used in the course of the budgetary consultations. i would also like to point out that a great many new measures have been introduced. the house has just held a debate on the new sixth framework programme for research and i am very pleased that a decision can now be taken and that, as i have heard, the vote on the sixth framework programme for research will take place tomorrow. the total amount of money available is very high, namely eur 17.5 billion until 2006. this should be emphasized again and again, because the european budget is often accused of failing to promote new developments by its special focus on agricultural policy. i think the research framework programme shows the opposite, that a really large sum is being invested in joint research funding and, once the decision has been taken, preparations for implementation can begin. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i would also like to mention another example of new measures, for example in the transport sector. eur 53 million is earmarked for the continuation of road safety measures. the 2003 budget year will also be marked by the establishment of the european aviation safety and maritime safety agencies. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the european union is able to meet the challenges of the future. that will be one of the tasks of the maritime safety agency. the new marco polo programme has also been included. these are community grants to improve the environmental performance of freight transport systems. finally, as an example of category 3, i would mention that the measures for a smart energy policy, namely the promotion of innovative forms of energy, have the highest rate of increase here, at almost 50%. i believe that this is yet another example of the priority given to promoting sustainable development being followed by appropriate financial approaches. i would now like to turn to the area of foreign policy, category 4. the appropriations for commitments are eur 4.9 billion. this represents an increase of 2.3% compared with the current financial year. the commission's draft is consistent with the priorities and commitments we have made in our foreign policy. i would like to mention the balkans. the commission has already proposed a number of measures to help the poorest countries in the world. the aid to afghanistan is also in line with the commitments made by the commission on behalf of the european union in tokyo in december 2001. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i would also like to stress that the mediterranean programme is to receive a 4.2% increase in funding. this includes pre-accession aid for turkey, malta and cyprus, but also new funds for the commitment made in the barcelona process to provide additional funds from the european investment bank, i.e. to set up a special facility. the commission proposes to set aside 25 million for this purpose, to finance, for example, a venture capital programme. the commission's foreign policy focuses on the fight against serious communicable diseases in many parts of the world. the commission's proposal will increase the budget for health measures by some 55 million in 2003, of which 35 million will be earmarked for a further contribution to the world health fund. i would also like to mention, in the field of foreign policy, above all that the commission is proposing to allocate eur 40 million to the common foreign and security policy. the commission has also proposed that the european parliament should be consulted on the draft budget. it is clear from this that this new form of activities of the common foreign and security policy is also part-financed by the operational part of the community budget. the commission has already made a number of proposals in this area. the average estimates are faced with a special situation here. on the one hand, we have a very high increase in pension expenditure. on the other hand, we have not made provision in the financial planning for the fact that the institutions of the european community - that is, both parliament and the commission, the european court of justice and the council - will of course also have to take preparatory measures in view of enlargement. the commission's preliminary draft budget therefore exceeds the ceiling, and specifically only for these preparatory measures. the commission proposes to make the necessary funds available from the flexibility instrument. i am sure that this will be one of the main points of discussion with the council as well. the council itself has envisaged an increase of 11% in its human resources. that is not the increase the commission is proposing. but i believe that it is in this area in particular that we must have a very concrete joint debate on how to take the necessary preparatory measures to make enlargement a success, also in the institutional sense. i expect that this will now be a focal point in the debate, as will, of course, the question of financing any new requirements in the field of foreign policy. i would like to mention palestine and cyprus as a reference. i hope we can find common the commission is not prepared to accept this. - mr president, commissioner, the report on the annual assessment of the implementation of the stability and convergence programmes which i am presenting to you and which was unanimously adopted by the economic and monetary affairs committee of the european parliament finds that the annual stability and convergence programmes submitted by the governments of the member states and their implementation in 2001 were for the most part consistent with the requirements of the stability and growth pact, despite the unfavourable economic conditions which prevailed due to a serious slowdown in growth rates, the temporary problems of the us economy and unforeseen events such as the terrorist attacks of 11 september. this generally positive picture, despite some more serious deviations in the case of germany and portugal, shows that the stability pact and its strict application, to which the policy of the european central bank has contributed, are a decisive factor in monetary stability. it has enabled the successful introduction of the euro and is widely regarded as a key condition for the european union's economic development to be based on sound and secure foundations, but this is still a question of time. unfortunately, growth rates, with few exceptions, remain low, unemployment is stagnant and high, with serious problems for the poorest and particularly the young, and inflationary pressures continue to threaten us. the european commission and ecofin believe that the recovery is coming and will be more evident in the last quarter of this year. the new stability programmes for the next three years also present a similar picture. it remains to be verified. unfortunately, despite the upturn in the us economy and some recent stock market booms, uncertainties remain and widespread popular discontent is sometimes expressed in extreme ways, particularly in the last elections. it is clear that simple management in the governance of countries is not enough. more effective economic policies are needed to strengthen the competitiveness of the european economy, to promote convergence, to encourage productive investment, especially high-tech, and to increase employment in order to address the concerns raised by the unfortunately uncontrolled influx of migrants and the crime attributed to them, as well as the persistently high unemployment, especially among young people. although some people are calling for the stability pact to be revised or not strictly applied, the committee on economic and monetary affairs considers it absolutely necessary to comply with it and therefore criticises the timid approach taken to the derogations observed in germany and portugal. unfortunately, despite the commission's recommendations to give these countries an early warning, as was done in the past for ireland, ecofin did not do so, apparently for political reasons. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. and the mistake is even more serious because in portugal, for example, after the elections, the new government revealed that it had concealed from the previous government the country's much worse fiscal situation, since the budget deficit tends to exceed by far the 3% threshold in relation to gross domestic product. the commission has also been asked to provide a report on the situation in the community, which is a very important one, and to draw up a report on the situation in the community. the above, which is clearly the result of so-called creative accounting, should stop and the commission is already pushing for more transparency and honesty in the data communicated by governments, while at the same time emphasizing the urgency of accelerating the necessary structural changes, especially in the pension sector, the liberalisation of markets, especially labour and energy, and the upgrading of the training provided in order to achieve the lisbon objectives. the european union must dare to do more, to learn from the successes of the american model, which yesterday, however, caused us problems by announcing an increase in agricultural subsidies by 80%, and without abandoning the social market economy, to leave the creative forces of private initiative unhindered but not, of course, unrewarded, in order to escape from the present uncertainty that feeds not a few threats. the european union must not allow itself to be overwhelmed by the threat of terrorism, which is a threat to the peace and stability of europe, and which is a threat to the stability of the world economy.
in writing. - (el) the report and the motion for a resolution on a european strategy for tourism - a sector which is particularly important for the economy and employment in the european union but also singularly neglected - contain many positive points, and that is why i am voting in favour. i would like to add the following: firstly, the european union must return to the development of a specific programme for tourism, as in the case of the philoxenia project, which was opposed by some member states. secondly, support for small and mediumsized enterprises in tourist regions, which are at risk of being stifled by the policies pursued at their expense by the dominant tour operators in the european union, must be a specific objective of social policy; thirdly, we need a broader policy which combines tourism development with respect for the historical heritage, aesthetic aspects and traditions of each tourist region, which are already being undermined by the industrial and standardized development of tourism.
in writing. - (el) the motion for a resolution by the committee on culture, youth, education, the media and sport is distinguished by its good intentions. but it is doubtful whether it can be effective in transforming the negative context that has developed for young people at european level, which is reflected either in the indifference of young people to the processes of european integration or in the protests of the most motivated groups of young people at european councils. such a transformation cannot be based simply on piecemeal and special measures but on a reorientation of community policies - from budgetary discipline to employment, from privatisation to support for public education, from repression to a framework to support young people entering the cycle of drug and alcohol dependence, from the creation of a fortress europe to the integration of immigrant children.
the commission. - mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i have just received a letter from the president of the commission. the member raises the question of the protection of minors in sport. it refers in particular to the risks posed to minors, often professionals or semi-professionals, by participation in major sports competitions. the issue of the protection of young sportsmen was therefore addressed for the first time in the declaration on the specific characteristics of sport and its social function in europe to be taken into account in the framework of common policies, adopted by the european council in nice. the commission addressed this issue in the context of the helsinki report on sport and the european sports forum held in lille on 26 and 27 october 2000, in particular at the workshop on youth protection, and the special situation of young people under 18 was also examined in detail during the discussions between the commission and fifa on the rules governing international transfers of football players, which were concluded in march 2001. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's sporting activities are not disrupted by the introduction of new technologies. the commission has, however, made a major contribution to the intergovernmental dialogue on this problem through its initiatives. in addition, measures have been taken at community level, taking into account the principle of subsidiarity. for example, on 22 june 1994 the council adopted directive 94/33 on the protection of young people at work. this directive prohibits child labour, regulates the work of young people and generally aims to ensure that every employer guarantees working conditions appropriate to the age of the young person. the main objective of the directive is to promote better working conditions for young people without imposing an absolute ban. finally, the commission has launched a number of anti-doping measures, particularly among young people. in 1999, a report on a plan for the community's contribution to the fight against doping was adopted. in addition, several studies were financed in the previous year, including one on the protection of young athletes. finally, the commission is currently considering the implementation of a new community anti-doping plan which will take even more account of the problem of combating doping among young people.
- i am aware of all that has been done in the past, but as we are facing an olympic organization, i think it is very important to see whether all these individual measures and studies will lead us either to a real community action plan or, if there is an encouragement from the commission to the member states and a decision to this effect, to national action plans on this particular subject. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. it is a real threat to the health of children, i believe, but it is also contrary to the spirit of sport for all and of voluntary sport.

and long term. the commission has called for the implementation of national action plans to combat discrimination in all candidate countries with a significant roma population. that's what happened. various eu programmes have made a significant contribution to this. but i must say very clearly that our political message to the candidate countries is that they are the ones who have the responsibility for improving the situation of the roma. we must not absolve these countries of their responsibility for this problem. in july 2000, the european union adopted binding legislation prohibiting racial and ethnic discrimination in the workplace, education, social services, etc. a second directive prohibits discrimination in the workplace on grounds of religion or belief, disability, age and sexual orientation. member states must transpose these directives into national law by 2003, which means that future new member states must also transpose these directives before they join the european union. the legal framework is flanked by an action programme, endowed with 100 million euro, which aims to improve our ability to combat racism and all other forms of discrimination. the commission also presented a proposal for a council framework decision in november 2001 to create a legal instrument in the field of criminal law. this legal instrument is designed to ensure that racist and xenophobic behaviour is punished in all member states with the same level of penalties, where the facts are the same. secondly, to improve and promote judicial cooperation in this area.

in writing. - (de) overall economic growth in the eu fell in 2001 to 1.6% (from 3.3% in 2000), while the unemployment rate remained virtually constant (7.7% in december 2001 compared to 7.9% in december 2000), the inflation rate increased with the introduction of the euro from 2.1% in december 2001 to 2.5% in january 2002, the share of investment in european gdp is well below the threshold required for sustainable growth. the rapporteur calls for strict compliance with the stability and convergence pact as a value in itself, instead of public investment, a vigorous programme on employment policy, and sound concepts on the guarantee of health care and pension schemes. its proposals to guarantee these same things are as outdated as they are fallacious: it calls for a categorical delimitation of expenditure (naturally savings must be made in particular on social benefits) instead of an improvement in revenue, it calls for moderate wage agreements for real wage income in overall decline, while, at the same time, transnational groups are making exponential profits and do not have to pay taxes in many cases. how can such a strategy encourage demand? i cannot approve such a report.
in writing. - (el) the report is based on the strict application of the stability pact, which has led to a deterioration in the living conditions of large sections of the population and, despite the proclamations, has not succeeded in bringing the european economy out of the crisis and ensuring high growth rates. it reinforces the anti-people policy of constantly increasing profits, with proposals aimed at diverting attention from the ultra-neo-liberal economic and social policy of the european union and its tragic consequences. as the socioeconomic situation worsens and inequalities multiply, the policy of structural reforms is strongly encouraged, which spreads unemployment, generalises flexible forms of work, uncertainty and lack of security for workers, generates poverty, misery and marginalisation for millions of citizens, reduces the cost of wages, ensuring a constant increase in capital profits. in the name of budgetary balance, there is a call for the dismantling of pension schemes to be accelerated and for markets to be fully liberalised, guaranteeing the impunity of monopolies. the calls for budgetary discipline represent an attempt to justify the continuing decline in spending on social protection, education, health and public services of general interest, while spending to strengthen big capital is maintained in full and overwhelming tax provisions are strengthened at the expense of workers. we vote against the resolution because the policies of the eu and the member states are contrary to the needs of workers, peasants, the lower and middle classes, and are subject to the choices of big business, whose interests they serve. we stand with the social strata who are resisting and fighting to defeat the ruthless economic and social policy imposed by the stability pact.

council. - the conclusions of the tampere european council of 15 and 16 october 1999, which aimed at implementing the provisions of title iv of the treaty, aimed at the progressive achievement of an area of freedom, security and justice, affirmed the need to develop a common policy on asylum and immigration. the conclusions also indicated the four elements on which such a policy should be based: cooperation with countries of origin, a common european asylum system, the management of migration flows and fair treatment for third-country nationals. in line with the tampere conclusions, the commission adopted two communications in november 2000, one on a common immigration policy and the other on a common asylum policy, in which it sets out the guidelines on which it considers that a common medium- and long-term strategy should be defined and a common framework created. the conclusions of the laeken european council of 14 and 15 december 2001 underlined the need, in line with the establishment and implementation of a common policy on asylum and immigration, as referred to in the conclusions of the tampere european council, to give impetus to the proposals under discussion in the council and to consider the adoption of further actions and measures as soon as possible. in this connection, the council bodies are currently examining various proposals for legislative acts. as regards legal immigration, the debates concern very important and sensitive issues, such as family reunification of third-country nationals and the status of those third-country nationals who have resided in a member state for many years and the requirements for third-country nationals to be admitted to the member states and to be able to pursue an economic activity there. as regards asylum, although discussions are still ongoing on certain proposals for directives, it was possible to reach a general agreement at the council on 25 and 26 april, pending the opinion of the european parliament, on such an important instrument as the directive on minimum standards for the reception of asylum seekers in the member states. as regards strengthening border controls and combating illegal immigration and trafficking in human beings, the council of 27 and 28 february 2002 adopted an action plan setting out the actions and measures to be taken by the union and the deadlines for their implementation. reference should also be made to the work of the high level group on asylum and migration, which was instructed by the council on 25 and 26 january 1999 to draw up action plans for the countries of origin and transit of asylum seekers and migrants, using an integrated approach. these action plans set out, inter alia, actions to promote and encourage the development of the countries concerned, and specific measures to reduce illegal migration flows. the council adopted action plans for afghanistan, albania - the border region -, morocco, somalia, sri lanka and iraq. in this connection, it is also worth recalling the establishment of a budget line, b7-667, to finance projects in the framework of cooperation with third countries in the field of migration. the difficulties experienced in the fields of immigration and asylum are largely due to the significant differences between national legislation in this field, which has also undergone profound changes in recent years. in addition, the need to prevent and combat illegal immigration has sometimes resulted in the adoption of more restrictive national regulations. however, despite the difficulties and problems encountered, discussions on the instruments envisaged and proposed for implementing the provisions of the treaty on the basis, in particular, of the guidelines set out in the conclusions of the tampere european council, usually highlight the commitment and efforts made at council level to speed up their examination and adoption and thus to create the basis for a body of rules binding on the community as a whole in this respect.

- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the result of the regulations adopted under agenda 2000 is extremely negative for farmers. the recent revisions have increased the co-responsibility fines and kept the quotas at a low level in relation to needs and actual production, causing a collapse in prices and farm income. the intervention prices and minimum prices were abolished or reduced, leaving small and mediumsized farmers to be exploited by the speculation of unpaid industrialists. decisions have been taken, such as the abolition of tobacco subsidies, which will lead to the desertification of large areas of the mediterranean countries, the poorest in the european union. the whole food and production chain is being distorted to the benefit of the large food industries, with the known food scandals, and no substantial proposal is being made to restructure crop production or to deal effectively with phenomena such as spongiform encephalopathy. the reversal of this dire situation can only be achieved if, with the mid-term review of the cap, the principles and guidelines of agenda 2000 are revised. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's agricultural policy is not disrupted by the introduction of new forms of production, which are not only the result of the introduction of new forms of production, but also of the introduction of new forms of production.
- mr president, commissioner, we all accept that information is a basic principle of democracy. free access, transparency and dissemination of information on the environment are fundamental rights of every citizen concerned about the quality of the environment in which he lives and works. access to data held by public authorities and related to the environment is essential not only to improve public awareness but also to ensure citizens' trust and democratic participation. the council has adopted very few amendments to the common position adopted by parliament in its plenary session. we therefore consider it necessary to bring back to the second reading all the important amendments which deal with issues such as, in principle, exemptions. it is important, i think, to limit the exceptions to the minimum possible, so that all those concerned can have access to a wide range of information. the commission has also proposed that the commission should be able to provide the member states with the information they require in order to ensure that the environmental impact assessment is carried out in a timely manner. another important point is the fee for the cost of providing the information. i believe that it should be limited to the real cost of reproduction, so that public access to information is facilitated as much as possible and that cost is in no way a deterrent to the acquisition of environmental information. i hope that our comments will be welcomed by the european commission and that we will move as quickly as possible towards the ratification of the aarhus treaty. finally, mr president, i would like to say on behalf of my political group that we fully support the report by mrs korhola, the rapporteur, whom i congratulate on her substantial work.
the committee on women's rights and equal opportunities, draftsman of the opinion, madam president, the green paper is a real challenge for business and society at large, since it aims to be at the heart of our efforts to improve our economic performance, modernise our social model and promote the european values of social solidarity and responsibility. the green paper wants to make it clear, and we agree with it, that the link between economic activity and success must promote social responsibility, which in turn contributes to the continued success of the enterprise and to the revitalization of the economy and society. it is therefore important to convince businesses, the social partners and society at large to look at social responsibility not as a casual activity but as a long-term strategy. this strategy must be fully linked to the quality of women's work, their professional integration and promotion, because these objectives have been recognised as being of primary importance for achieving the lisbon strategic objectives. we therefore believe that this policy must be based on information, awareness, voluntary work and responsibility. all the measures to be implemented must include the implementation of the laws and the introduction of the community acquis on equality between men and women, but also more, relating to initiatives to facilitate the harmonization of working and family life, exploiting women's potential in the design and implementation of policies within the enterprise, upgrading the image of women. we also believe that the forum proposed by the european parliament should take seriously not only these issues in its deliberations but also the participation of women in its work.
in writing. - (el) the aim of the belgian-spanish initiative and the report under consideration is to be able to amend the europol convention in such a way that this service becomes 'operational', without approval or ratification by the national parliaments. it is clear that the competences of europol as laid down in the constituent convention (collection, processing and exchange of information) no longer meet the current needs of the eu, i.e. the acceleration of the establishment of mechanisms of militarization and repression. moreover, it is not enough that its substantive jurisdiction has been increased so that there is no serious crime (including so-called terrorism) that does not concern it. under the pretext of the events of 11 september, there is once again an attempt to give europol powers of a clearly police and law enforcement nature, alongside or above the respective national agencies, such as the famous fbi. to do that, we have to change the convention. the belgian-spanish initiative proposes to do so by decision (unanimous) of the council, while the deprez report goes further by proposing that the convention be transformed into a 'decision' of the council, so that it can be amended at any time and simply by a qualified majority of the council. meps from the communist party of greece voted against both texts because they aim to make europol conform to the need to strengthen the mechanisms of repression, restrict democratic freedoms and persecute popular movements.
in writing. - (el) despite the efforts made by the eu to present itself in a different light from the us, the eu-latin america summit revealed few differences between the eu and the us, except in the context of the internal contradictions of imperialism. the joint communiqué makes no mention of the us blockade of cuba, the interruption of the peace process in colombia and the us 'colombia' plan and its extension. there is no reference to the attempted coup in venezuela and the eu does not even consider that it needs to justify its initial position vis-à-vis the coup plotters. in addition, support for anti-people government measures in argentina and fear that argentina is escaping the imf's grip is clearly visible. it also seems that there is an attempt to facilitate even more the penetration of european capital into latin america. the eu is simply proposing neoliberal policies and free trade agreements to these countries instead of helping them to build their economies, and is making a concerted effort to involve them in the so-called fight against terrorism, ignoring the voices raised at the porto alegre forum and the sao paulo parliamentary forum. the mass demonstrations in madrid have revealed the deep exasperation and combative determination of the people against the europe of capital. the peoples of latin america have nothing positive to expect from the eu if it does not in any way counterbalance the united states.
in writing. - (el) the rapporteur considers the reduction in what is commonly called the first pillar - agricultural production - to be a given fact and proposes to mitigate the consequences of this reduction by strengthening the second pillar, rural development. in an attempt to deal with the negative situation, the report proposes to reduce aid granted to the first pillar on the basis of 'eco-conditionality' criteria. in other words, under the guise of ecology, it proposes to abolish aid and transfer it to the second pillar in order to respect budgetary discipline. this logic is tantamount to opening pandora's box, because it will make it possible to remove subsidies for a series of products, after pseudo-scientific 'studies' have judged them harmful to the environment. finally, as regards enlargement, the report follows the logic of the 10-year transitional period for the candidate countries, thus advocating a two-tier cap: some countries have rights and duties, others have reduced duties and rights. the report is an attempt to celebrate agenda 2000 and enlargement, which is taking place under conditions unacceptable to the candidate countries. we will vote against the report and we will fight alongside the small and mediumsized farmers who are trying to overturn everything that the rapporteur takes for granted and that she is trying to encourage, this anti-agricultural policy which inevitably leads to the extinction and bankruptcy of the european peasantry.
